, Proposition 4.5]
satisfy the following properties.

(1)

The flow
\mathcal{W}_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}
has the form
\xi\varrho^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}
for some vector
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}^{3}\cap\mathbb{S}^{2}
and a scalar function
\varrho^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}
. There exists a pairwise symmetric tensor potential
\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2^{\mathsf{d}}}
such that
\varrho^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=\lambda^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathrm{div}^{D}(\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k})
.

Then there exists
\mathcal{U}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
if
\{\xi,\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime}\}\subset\mathbb{Q}^{3}\cap\mathbb{S}^{2}
form an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\xi\times\xi^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime\prime}
, then we have
12
12

12

The double index
ii
indicates that
\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{D}}-2}\left(\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}\right)
is a
2
-tensor, and we are summing over the diagonal components. The factor of

appears because each component on the diagonal of this
3\times 3
matrix is
\Delta^{-1}\varrho_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}
. The formula then follows from the identity
\mathrm{curl\,}\mathrm{curl\,}=-\Delta
for divergence-free vector fields.

\mathcal{U}_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}=-\frac{1}{3}\xi^{\prime}\underbrace{\lambda^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\xi^{\prime\prime}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{D}}-2}\left(\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}\right)\right)^{ii}}_{=:\varphi_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{\prime\prime k}}+\frac{1}{3}\xi^{\prime\prime}\underbrace{\lambda^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{D}}-2}\left(\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{k}\right)\right)^{ii}}_{=:\varphi_{\xi,\lambda,r}^{\prime k}}\,,

(3.5)

and thus

\mathrm{curl\,}\mathcal{U}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=\xi\lambda^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{D}}\left(\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}\right)=\xi\varrho^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=:\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}\,,

(3.6)

and

\xi\cdot\nabla\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda,r}=(\xi\cdot\nabla)\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=(\xi\cdot\nabla)\mathcal{U}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=0\,.

(3.7)

(2)

The sets of functions

when

and same holds for

.

(3)

\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}
is a stationary, pressureless solution to the Euler equations.

(4)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}=\xi\otimes\xi}

For all

{\left\|\nabla^{n}\vartheta^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda^{n}r^{\left(\frac{2}{p}-1\right)}},\qquad{\left\|\nabla^{n}\varrho^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\lambda^{n}r^{\left(\frac{2}{p}-1\right)}}

(7)

\displaystyle=x\,,

3.2 Cutoff functions

We introduce and recall basic facts about time cutoffs, flow maps, intermittent pressure cutoffs, mildly and strongly anisotropic checkerboard cutoff functions, velocity cutoff functions, and cutoff aggregation lemmas. Further details are contained in each subsubsection.

3.2.1 Time cutoffs

We introduce two collections of basic temporal cutoffs, which will be useful for estimating flow maps defined on various regions of space-time. We refer the reader to
[
25
, subsection 5.1]
, for further details (although these are the simplest cutoffs contained in this manuscript and do not require sophisticated definitions). The first set of temporal cutoff functions
\chi_{i,k,q}(t)
satisfies

\displaystyle t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\iff

\displaystyle t\in\left[(k-1)\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2},(k+1)\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}\right]\,,

(3.14a)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{m}\chi_{i,k,q}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Gamma_{q}^{i+2}\tau_{q}^{-1})^{m}\qquad\textnormal{for }m\geq 0\,,

(3.14b)

\displaystyle\chi_{i,k_{1},q}(t)\chi_{i,k_{2},q}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv 0\qquad\qquad\textnormal{unless }|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 1\,

(3.14c)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi_{i,k,q}^{6}

\displaystyle\equiv 1\,.

(3.14d)

The second set of temporal cutoff functions
\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
has a slightly longer timescale and is only needed for technical reasons; see Lemma
4.5
. They are supported in the set of times
t
satisfying

\left|t-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}k\right|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i}\,.

(3.15)

In addition, if
(i,k)
and
({i^{*}},{k^{*}})
are such that
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\neq\emptyset
and
{i^{*}}\in\{i-1,i,i+1\}
, then as a consequence of these definitions and a sufficiently large choice of
\lambda_{0}
, we have that
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\widetilde{\chi}_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
.

3.2.2 Velocity cutoffs

Recall from subsection
2.3
that one should heuristically think of
\psi_{i,q}
as the characteristic function of the set where
|\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}|\approx\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i}
. The most important properties of the velocity cutoffs are the partition of unity property in (
2.13
), the maximum value of
i
in (
2.14
), the derivative bounds for the cutoffs in (
2.41
), and the estimate (
2.29
), which controls a weighted sum of velocity cutoffs (and by extension
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
itself) by a pressure increment. We will construct the new velocity cutoffs
\psi_{i,{q+\bar{n}}}
in Proposition
3.24
after constructing the velocity increment in Definition
3.22
.

3.2.3 Estimates on flow maps

The first lemma and corollary in this subsubsection assert that forwards and backwards Lagrangian trajectories of the velocity field
\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
restricted to the local Lipschitz timescale determined by the cutoff functions stay contained in the support of a few neighboring velocity cutoff functions. The results are discussed further in
[
25
, Section 5.2]
.

Lemma 3.5

(
Lagrangian paths don’t jump many supports).

Let
q^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
and
(x_{0},t_{0})
be given. Assume that the index
i
is such that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}^{2}(x_{0},t_{0})\geq\kappa^{2}
, where
\kappa\in\left[\frac{1}{16},1\right]
. Then the forward flow
(X(t),t):=(X(x_{0},t_{0};t),t)
of the velocity field
\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
originating at
(x_{0},t_{0})
has the property that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}^{2}(X(t),t)\geq\nicefrac{{\kappa^{2}}}{{2}}
for all
t
such that
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i+4}
.

Corollary 3.6

(
Backwards Lagrangian paths don’t jump many supports).

Suppose
(x_{0},t_{0})
is such that
\psi^{2}_{i,q^{\prime}}(x_{0},t_{0})\geq\kappa^{2}
, where
\kappa\in\left[\nicefrac{{1}}{{16}},1\right]
. For
\left|t-t_{0}\right|\leq\tau_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i+3}
, define
x
to satisfy
x_{0}=X(x,t;t_{0})
. Then we have that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}(x,t)\neq 0
.

We can now define and estimate the flow of the vector field
\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}
for
q^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
on the support of a velocity and time cutoff function. These estimates are also contained in
[
25
, subsection 5.2]
and are proven in a similar manner to all such estimates for convex integration schemes.

Definition 3.7

(
Flow maps).

We define
\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(x,t)=\Phi_{(i,k)}(x,t)
by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q^{\prime}}\cdot\nabla)\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}=0\\
\Phi_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(x,k{\tau_{q^{\prime}}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-i-2})=x\,.\end{array}\right.

(3.16)

We denote the inverse of the gradient
D\Phi_{(i,k)}
by
(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
, in contrast to
D\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
, which is the gradient of
\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
.

Corollary 3.8

(
Deformation bounds).

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}
,
q^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
, and
2\leq N\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have the following bounds on the set
\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k}:=\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q^{\prime}}(x,t){\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q^{\prime}}(t)}
.

\displaystyle\left\|D\Phi_{(i,k)}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}

(3.17a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|D^{N}\Phi^{-1}_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}+\left\|D^{N-1}\left((D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N-1}

(3.17b)

Furthermore, we have the following bounds for
1\leq N+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
and
0\leq N^{\prime}\leq N
:

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}+1}\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\leq(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}\tau_{q^{\prime}}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q^{\prime}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}\right)

(3.18a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q^{\prime}}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}}(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{i,q^{\prime},k})}

\displaystyle\leq(\lambda_{q^{\prime}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{i}\tau_{q^{\prime}}^{-1},{\mathrm{T}}_{q^{\prime}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}\right)\,.

(3.18b)

3.2.4 Intermittent pressure cutoffs

In this subsubsection, we record the main properties of the cutoff functions for the intermittent pressure
\pi_{\ell}
obtained from mollifying
\pi_{q}^{q}
(see the mollification estimates in Lemma
3.33
). These cutoff functions are rigorously defined and estimated in
[
25
, Section 5.3]
. The definition is essentially a level-set chopping of
\pi_{\ell}
, which we now discuss. We shall need estimates for derivatives of error terms on the support of a particular cutoff, and for the derivatives of the cutoff itself; such estimates follow from the pointwise nature of the bounds in (
2.20c
) and (
2.26
). These pointwise bounds follow from the construction of the intermittent pressure; see the pointwise bounds in Proposition
A.13
, (
A.57
) and (
A.58
). These bounds in turn follow from the definition of the pressure increment
\sigma_{H}
; for example let us consider a Reynolds stress error term
H=G\rho\circ\Phi
. Here
G
oscillates at a low spatial frequency
\lambda_{G}
and a Lagrangian frequency
\nu
(corresponding to a material derivative operator
D_{t}
) and has
L^{p}
norm size
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}
for
p=\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}},\infty
,
\Phi
is a flow map, and
\rho
oscillates at a high frequency
\lambda_{\rho}
and has
L^{p}
norm size
\mathcal{C}_{\rho,p}
for
p=\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}},\infty
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho):=\left(\left(\mathcal{C}_{\rho,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\right)^{2}+\sum_{N=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}(\lambda_{\rho}\Gamma)^{-2N}|D^{N}\rho|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathcal{C}_{\rho,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\,,

(3.19a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Pi}(G):=\left(\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\right)+\sum_{N=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\sum_{M=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}(\lambda_{G}\Gamma)^{-2N}(\nu\Gamma)^{-2M}|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\,,

(3.19b)

\displaystyle\sigma_{H}=\mathsf{\Pi}(G)\left(\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)\right)\circ\Phi=:\sigma_{H}^{+}-\sigma_{H}^{-}\,.

(3.19c)

We pause to highlight a few aspects of this definition. First,
\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)
and
\mathsf{\Pi}(G)
are supported on the supports of
\rho
and
G
, respectively, and scale linearly with
\rho
and
G
.
13
13

13

Stress errors and pressure errors have the same scaling. Current errors, however, scale like
(\textnormal{pressure})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
. Therefore to make a pressure increment for a current error, one uses
L^{1}
norms instead of
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
norms and then raises the sum to the
\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}
power, and the
\mathcal{C}_{\rho,1}
to the
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
power, and similarly for
G
.
Second, note that
\sigma_{H}
is effectively mean zero, so that we may invert the divergence. Next, we point out that the portion
\sigma_{H}^{-}
depends only on the mean of
\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)
but not any further properties of
\rho
, and is therefore effectively low frequency. Therefore, we may actually dominate
\sigma_{H}^{-}
by
anticipated
pressure, i.e. rescaled versions of old, lower frequency, intermittent pressure; see the bounds in (
3.60f
), (
3.82d
), (
3.97e
), and (
5.13e
). Finally,
\sigma_{H}^{+}
incorporates a weighted sum of at most
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
material derivatives and
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
spatial derivatives, where the weight uses a small factor
\Gamma
(typically
\Gamma_{q}
). The effect of
\Gamma
in the sum is that the terms for
N
and
M
very large are actually quite
small
, since
G
for example oscillates at frequency
\lambda_{G}
, which is less than
\lambda_{G}\Gamma
. This implies that in fact for
N
and
M
larger than
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
and
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
,

\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}H\right|\lesssim\left(\delta_{\rm tiny}+\sigma_{H}^{+}\right)(\lambda_{G}\Gamma)^{N}(\nu\Gamma)^{M}\,,\qquad\qquad\mbox{where}\quad\delta_{\rm tiny}\ll 1\,.

That is,
\sigma_{H}^{+}
dominates
H
and essentially
all
of its derivatives using a slightly weakened multiplicative derivative cost, up to a negligible constant
\delta_{\rm tiny}
. In a similar way, we can also obtain

\displaystyle|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\sigma_{H}^{+}|\lesssim\left(\delta_{\rm tiny}+\sigma_{H}^{+}\right)(\lambda_{G}\Gamma)^{N}(\nu\Gamma)^{M}\,,

which implies that
\sigma_{H}^{+}
dominates essentially all of its derivatives.

We now recall from
[
25
, Section 5.3]
the key properties of the intermittent stress cutoffs. The properties below include a partition of unity property, a comparability estimate to
\pi_{\ell}
itself, control on the maximum index
{j_{\rm max}}
used in the cutoffs, derivative estimates, and Lebesgue norms.

Lemma 3.9

(
Intermittent pressure cutoffs).

There exist cutoff functions
\{\omega_{j,q}^{{6}}\}_{j\geq 0}
satisfying the following properties.

(i)

We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\omega_{j,q}^{{6}}(t,x)\equiv 1\,,\qquad\omega_{j,q}\omega_{j^{\prime},q}\equiv 0\quad\textnormal{if}\quad|j-j^{\prime}|>1\,.

(3.20)

(ii)

We have the comparability properties

\displaystyle\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}(\omega_{j,q})}\pi_{\ell}

(3.21a)

\displaystyle\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}\sum_{j}\omega_{j,q}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}

\displaystyle\leq\pi_{\ell}\,.

(3.21b)

(iii)

There exists
{j_{\rm max}}={j_{\rm max}}(q)=\inf\left\{j\,:\,\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{2j}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}\geq\Gamma_{q}^{3+\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}\right\}
which is bounded independently of
q
, and

\displaystyle\omega_{j,q}\equiv 0\qquad\mbox{for all}\qquad j>{j_{\rm max}}\,.

(3.22)

Moreover, we have the bound

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q}^{2j_{\rm max}}\leq\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}+6}\,.

(3.23)

(iv)

For
q\geq 0
,
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, and
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}}|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\omega_{j,q}|}{\omega_{j,q}^{1-(N+M)/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\lesssim(\Gamma_{q}^{5}\Lambda_{q})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+4}\tau_{q}^{-1},{\mathrm{T}}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.24)

(v)

For any
r\geq\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}
and
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\omega_{j,q}\right\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{3(1-j)}{r}}\,.

(3.25)

3.2.5 Mildly and strongly anisotropic checkerboard cutoffs

We now introduce the mildly and strongly anisotropic cutoffs, which localize the support of the intermittent Mikado bundles defined in (
3.2
). These cutoffs are always composed with flow maps
\Phi_{i,k,q}
, which ensures that the cutoffs retain their general shape and dimensions on the local Lipschitz timescale. In addition, a number of summability properties are imposed, which help ensure the quadratic and cubic cancellation properties and more; see subsection
5.2
. Finally, the cutoffs satisfy spatial and material derivative estimates. Of particular importance is that the anisotropic shape of the cutoffs is adapted to a particular bundle
\mathbb{B}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
, so that the spatial derivative in the (flowed)
\xi
direction satisfies a better estimate. This is crucial in subsection
5.2
, for example; see (
5.9
), in which the divergence does
not
land on the costliest objects, due to the fact that the derivative is in the (flowed)
\xi
direction. The anisotropy also helps limit the size of the support on which a bundle exists, aiding in the placement; see section
4
. The proofs of these two lemmas are straightforward; we refer to
[
25
, Lemmas 5.14, 5.15]
for further details.

Lemma 3.10

(
Mildly anistropic checkerboard cutoffs).

Given
q
,
\xi\in\Xi\cup\Xi^{\prime}
,
i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exist cutoff functions

\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)=\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\left(\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t)\right)\,,

(3.26)

which satisfy the following properties.

(i)

At the time
\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i-2}
at which
\Phi_{i,k,q}
is the identity, the support of
\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}
is contained in a rectangular prism of dimensions no larger than
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
in the direction of
\xi_{z}
, and
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\Gamma_{q}^{5}(\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}
in the directions perpendicular to
\xi_{z}
.

(ii)

The material derivative
D_{t,q}(\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}})
vanishes.

(iii)

We have the summability properties for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
;

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}}\bigl(\zeta_{q,R,i,k,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv 1\,,

(3.27a)

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}\,:\,l=l_{0}}\zeta_{q,\varphi,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}^{2}(x,t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,l_{0}}^{4}(\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t))\,,

(3.27b)

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}\,:\,l^{\perp}=l^{\perp}_{0}}\zeta_{q,\varphi,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}^{3}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,l_{0}^{\perp}}^{3}(\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t))\,.

(3.27c)

Furthermore, at the time for which
\Phi_{i,k,q}
is the identity,
\sum\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left(\chi_{q,\xi,l_{0}^{\perp}}^{3}\right)
is a
\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}^{3}}}{{\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}}}
-periodic function which satisfies

\left\langle\sum_{l^{\perp}}\chi_{q,e_{1},l^{\perp}}^{3}(x_{2},x_{3})\right\rangle=c_{3}\,.

(3.28)

Here and in the rest of the article,
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes the spatial average over the periodic domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}
.

(iv)

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. Then for all
N_{1}+N_{2}+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N_{1}}D_{t,q}^{M}({\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}})^{N_{2}}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q}^{-5}\lambda_{q+1}\right)^{N_{1}}\left(\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}\right)^{N_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.29)

Lemma 3.11

(
Strongly anisotropic checkerboard cutoff function).

The cutoff functions
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
defined using
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
from Definition
3.2
satisfy the following properties:

(1)

The material derivative
D_{t,q}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)})
vanishes.

(2)

For all fixed values of
q,i,k,\xi
, each
t\in\mathbb{R}
, and all
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
,

\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)})^{6}(x,t)=1\,.

(3.30)

(3)

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. Then for all
N_{1}+N_{2}+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N_{1}}D_{t,q}^{M}({\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}})^{N_{2}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+\lfloor\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}\rfloor}^{N_{1}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.31)

(4)

For fixed
q,i,k,\xi
, we have that

\displaystyle\#\left\{(\vec{l},I):\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\zeta_{q,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{I}_{\xi}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\neq\emptyset\right\}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{2}\,.

(3.32)

3.2.6 Definition of the cumulative cutoff function

With all the various cutoffs functions defined previously, we introduce the cumulative cutoff functions

\displaystyle\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\,(x,t)

\displaystyle=\psi_{i,q}^{\diamond}(x,t)\omega_{j,q}^{\diamond}(x,t)\chi_{i,k,q}^{\diamond}(t)\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\,.

(3.33)

The
\diamond
in the superscript is equal to
2
if
\diamond=\varphi
(so that they are cubic-summable to
1
) and
3
if
\diamond=R
(so that they are square-summable to
1
). We have the following
L^{p}
estimates on
\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}
, which may be proven directly using (
3.25
) and (
2.18
); we refer to
[
25
, Lemma 5.17]
for details.

Lemma 3.12

(
Cumulative support bounds for cutoff functions).

For
r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with
\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}=1
and any
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j,\leq{j_{\rm max}}
,
\xi\in\Xi,\Xi^{\prime}
, and
\diamond=\varphi,R
, we have that for each
t
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{l}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}(t,x)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{-3i+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}{r_{1}}+\frac{-3j}{r_{2}}+3}\,.

(3.34)

We furthermore have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}}\approx\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}}\lesssim 1\,.

(3.35)

3.2.7 Cutoff aggregation lemmas

We now introduce several “aggregation lemmas.” The basic idea of these lemmas is that since specific error terms include various cutoffs, such as
\psi_{i,q}
,
\omega_{j,q}
,
\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}
, or
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}
, they must be estimated individually first. This produces bounds which depend on indices
i,j,\vec{l},I
. However, by summing over such indices, we may
aggregate
the localized estimates into global (and thus more useful) pointwise estimates and
L^{p}
estimates. The proofs of these aggregation estimates are quite short and follow from direct computation; we refer the reader to
[
25
, Section 5.6]
for details.

Corollary 3.13

(
Aggregated
L^{p}
estimates).

Let
\theta\in{(}0,3]
, and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\geq 0
with
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=\theta
. Let
H=H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}
or
H=H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}
be a function with

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\qquad\textnormal{or}\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,.

(3.36)

Let
p\in[1,{\infty)}
and let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,3]
be such that
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=\nicefrac{{3}}{{p}}
. Assume that there exists
\mathcal{C}_{H},N_{*},M_{*},N_{x},M_{t}
and
\lambda,\Lambda,\tau,\mathrm{T}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}(t,x)\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{C}_{H}\Gamma_{q}^{\theta_{1}i+\theta_{2}j}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)

(3.37a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\mathrm{supp\,}_{x}\left(\eta_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}(t,x)\right)\right|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{C}_{H}\Gamma_{q}^{\theta_{1}i+\theta_{2}j}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)\,

(3.37b)

for
N\leq N_{*},M\leq M_{*}
. Then in the same range of
N
and
M
,

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}\sum_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{3+\theta_{1}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}}\mathcal{C}_{H}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\tau^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+1},\mathrm{T}^{-1}\right)

(3.38a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}\sum_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}H_{i^{\prime},j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\right\|_{L^{p}}

3.3 Premollified velocity increment, premollified velocity increment potential, pressure increment, velocity increment, and new velocity cutoffs

We are now ready to define the premollified velocity increment
w_{q+1}
and premollified velocity increment potential
\upsilon_{q+1}
, which we do in the first two subsubsections. Then, we define the associated pressure increments and pressure current error. Next, we define the velocity increment, which we denote with
\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
, and the velocity increment potential, which we denote with
\widehat{\upsilon}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
. Finally, we define the new velocity cutoffs
\psi_{i,{q+\bar{n}}}
and record a useful estimate for the velocity cutoffs in terms of the velocity pressure increment.

3.3.1 Premollified velocity increment

The premollified velocity increment
w_{q+1}
will be divided into Reynolds and current correctors, and principal parts and divergence correctors:

\displaystyle w_{q+1}=w_{q+1,R}+w_{q+1,\varphi}\,,\qquad w_{q+1}^{(p)}:=w_{q+1,R}^{(p)}+w_{q+1,\varphi}^{(p)}\,,\qquad w_{q+1}^{(c)}:=w_{q+1,R}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,\varphi}^{(c)}\,.

(3.46)

We will use the intermittent bundles and pipe flows from subsection
3.1
to define
w_{q+1}
, which however requires choices of placement for both the bundling pipe
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}
and
\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
. We delay this choice until Section
4
, as many properties such as the basic estimates on the size of
w_{q+1}
do not depend on the choices of placement. The principal portion
w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(p)}
and divergence corrector portion
w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(c)}
,
\diamond=R,\varphi
by

\displaystyle w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{a_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{I,\diamond}_{\xi}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi),\diamond}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:w_{(\xi),\diamond}^{(p),I}}\,,

(3.47a)

\displaystyle w_{q+1,\diamond}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I}\underbrace{\nabla\left(a_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{I,\varphi}_{\xi}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{(\xi),\diamond}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:w_{(\xi),\diamond}^{(c),I}}\,.

(3.47b)

The coefficient functions

are defined by

\displaystyle a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},\varphi}

\displaystyle=a_{(\xi),\varphi}=\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\Gamma^{j-1}_{q}\psi_{i,q}^{\varphi}\omega_{j,q}^{\varphi}\chi_{i,k,q}^{\varphi}\zeta_{q,\varphi,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3j-3}_{q}}\right)\,,

For convenience we shall often suppress the indices

for

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi}\widetilde{\gamma}^{3}_{\xi}\left(\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3j-3}_{q}}\right)\xi=\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3j-3}_{q}}\,,

(3.50)

The coefficient functions satisfy the following estimates, which may be proved via the Fa’a di Bruno formula and direct computation. We refer the reader to

for further details on these estimates.

3.3.2 Premollified velocity increment potentials

We now introduce the premollified velocity increment potentials. The basic idea is that the premollified velocity increment is defined as a product of high-frequency and low-frequency functions, and therefore can be written as the divergence of a potential which gains a factor of the high frequency. In this case, the gain is
\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{-1}
, which comes from the thickness of the pipe. The premollified velocity increment potentials therefore satisfy a number of properties: their iterated divergence is equal to the premollified velocity increment, up to a small error which is negligible; they are supported in a neighborhood of the support of the premollified velocity increment itself; and each “inverse divergence” gains
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
. We refer to
[
25
, Lemma 10.1]
for more details. Note that item
iii
gives that
\|w_{q+1}\|_{3}\approx\delta_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
and
\|w_{q+1}\|_{\infty}\approx\Gamma_{q}^{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}}{{2}}}
, as expected.

Lemma 3.17

(
Premollified velocity increment potential).

For a given
w_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
,
\texttt{l}=p,c
, as in (
3.46
), there exists a tensor
\upsilon_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
and an error
e_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
such that the following hold.

(i)

Let
{\mathsf{d}}
be as in (
xi
). Then
w_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
can be written in terms of
\upsilon_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
and
e_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
as

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{({p})}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{d}}}\upsilon_{q+1}^{({p})}+e_{q+1}^{({p})}\,,\qquad w_{q+1}^{({c})}=\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{d}}}(r_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\upsilon_{q+1}^{({c})})+r_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}e_{q+1}^{({c})}\,,

(3.54)

or equivalently notated component-wise as
(w_{q+1}^{(p)})^{\bullet}=\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{\mathsf{d}}}\upsilon_{q+1}^{(p,\bullet,i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}+e_{q+1}^{\bullet}
.

(ii)

\upsilon_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
and
e_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}
have the support property
14
14

14

For any smooth set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
, we use
\Omega\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
to denote the set
\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}(\Omega)\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, i.e. the space-time set whose characteristic function is annihilated by
D_{t,q}
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\upsilon_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})}),\,\mathrm{supp\,}(e_{q+1}^{(\texttt{l})})

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\chi_{i,k,q}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho^{I}_{(\xi),\diamond},2\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,.

(3.55)

(iii)

For
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{d}}
,

,
15

15

If
k=0
, we adopt the convention that
\partial_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial_{i_{k}}
is the identity operator.
satisfies the estimates

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{{i,q}}D^{N}{D_{t,q}^{M}}\upsilon_{q+1,k}^{(\texttt{l})}\right\|_{3}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{10}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{q}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+14}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{8}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(3.56a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{{i,q}}D^{N}{D_{t,q}^{M}}\upsilon_{q+1,k}^{(\texttt{l})}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}}{2}+10}r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+14}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{8}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(3.56b)

for

Remark 3.18

(
Cumulative premollified velocity increment potential).

We let
\upsilon_{q+1}:=\upsilon_{q+1}^{(p)}+r_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\upsilon_{q+1}^{(c)}
and

\upsilon_{q+1,k}^{\bullet}:=\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\mathsf{d}}-k}\partial_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial_{i_{k}}\upsilon_{q+1}^{(\bullet,i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}
. As a corollary of Lemma
3.17
, we have that
w_{q+1}=\mathrm{div}^{{\mathsf{d}}}\upsilon_{q+1}+e_{q+1}
, where
\upsilon_{q+1}
and
e_{q+1}
satisfy the properties (
3.55
)–(
3.57
) (up to implicit constants).

3.3.3
Pressure increment
for premollified velocity increments

The definition of the velocity pressure increment
\sigma_{\upsilon}
is quite similar to that from (
3.19
), after noticing that the premollified velocity increment has a natural high/low frequency product structure; we refer to
[
25
, Proposition 7.3]
for details. The main difference in the definition is that the
square
of velocity scales like Reynolds stresses and pressure, so we do not need to take the
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
power on the sum like in (
3.19
). The most important properties of the velocity pressure increment are as follows. First, the square root of the positive portion of the pressure increment dominates the premollified velocity increment potential, as in (
3.58
); next, the velocity pressure increment
can be decomposed into “positive and negative portions,
\sigma_{\upsilon}^{\pm}
,” which satisfy estimates in (
3.60
) asserting, among other things, that
\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-}
is dominated by anticipated pressure; third, the positive portion of the velocity pressure increment obeys a good spatial support property in (
3.61
), which follows from (
3.55
) the results of section
4
; and finally, there exists a negligible function of time which handles the mean of
\sigma_{\upsilon}
in (
3.63
) (see (
3.67b
)). Details may be found in
[
25
, Lemma 10.4]
.

Lemma 3.19

(
Pressure increment).

For
\texttt{l}=p,c
, there exists a pressure increment
\sigma_{\upsilon^{(\texttt{l})}}=\sigma_{\upsilon^{(\texttt{l})}}^{+}-\sigma_{\upsilon^{(\texttt{l})}}^{-}
associated to the sum
\sum_{k=0}^{\mathsf{d}}\upsilon_{q+1,k}^{(\texttt{l})}
defined in Lemma
3.17
such that the following properties hold.

(i)

We have that for all
k=0,1,\dots,{\mathsf{d}}
and any
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{d}}
,
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\upsilon_{q+1,k}^{(\texttt{l})}\right|\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon^{(\texttt{l})}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.58)

(ii)

Set

\displaystyle\sigma_{\upsilon}^{\pm}:=\sigma_{\upsilon^{(p)}}^{\pm}+\sigma_{\upsilon^{(c)}}^{\pm}\,,\qquad\sigma_{\upsilon}=\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}-\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-}\,.

(3.59)

Then we have that for all
N\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
and
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

(3.60a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{-9}}\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,\quad

(3.60b)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,\quad

(3.60c)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{-9}}\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

(3.60d)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

(3.60e)

\displaystyle{\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-}\right|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi_{\ell}\Gamma_{q}^{30}{r_{q}^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}}(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.60f)

(iii)

We have that for
q+1\leq q^{\prime\prime}\leq{q+\bar{n}}-1
and
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+})\cap B(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime\prime}{+1}})\,,\quad\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{-})\cap B(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}{+1}})=\emptyset\,.

(3.61)

(iv)

Define

\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\upsilon}}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle D_{t,q}\sigma_{\upsilon}\right\rangle(s)\,ds\,.

(3.62)

Then we have that for
0\leq M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\upsilon}}\right|\leq(\max(1,T))^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.63)

Remark 3.20

(
Pointwise bounds for principal and corrector parts).

From (
3.54
)–(
3.57
), (
3.58
), and (
7.13a
), we have that for
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{q+1}^{(p)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon^{(p)}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

(3.64a)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{q+1}^{(c)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon^{(c)}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.64b)

Note that thanks to the factor
r_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}
in (
3.54
), the bound in (
3.64b
) has an extra gain compared to (
3.64a
). We may upgrade (
3.64
) by applying Lemma
A.3
, so that in the same range of
N
and
M
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,{q+\bar{n}}-1}D^{N}D_{t,{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{M}w_{q+1}^{(p)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon^{(p)}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}}})^{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{i-5},\mathrm{T}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{-1}\right)\,,

(3.65a)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,{q+\bar{n}}-1}D^{N}D_{t,{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{M}w_{q+1}^{(c)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon^{(c)}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{10}}}})^{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{i-5},\mathrm{T}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.65b)

Next, we recall that the pressure increment contributes to
\pi_{q}
and hence
\kappa_{q}
in (
2.8
), and thus one must account for the current error term
\phi_{\upsilon}\approx\mathrm{div}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left(D_{t,q}\sigma_{\upsilon}\right)
. The first step, roughly speaking, is to decompose
\sigma_{\upsilon}
using the synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}(\sigma_{\upsilon})+\left(\sum_{k=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2}^{{q+\bar{n}}+1}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(\lambda_{k-1},\lambda_{k}]}(\sigma_{\upsilon})\right)+\left(\mathrm{Id}-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}+1}}\right)(\sigma_{\upsilon})\,,

(3.66)

from subsection (
A.3
), obtaining (
3.67a
); the term
\sigma_{\upsilon}^{*}
comes from the last term in (
3.66
) and is negligibly small, since
\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}+1}>\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}
and we only expect these functions to oscillate at frequencies no larger than
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}
. Then after applying the localized inverse divergence in Proposition
A.7
to
D_{t,q}
of each of these terms, the important properties of these error terms are their pointwise estimates, and their spatial support properties, which may be obtained from Proposition
A.7
and the results of section
4
. We note that the pointwise estimates in (
3.68
) are in terms of
old
intermittent pressure, which prevents a loop of pressure creation and new current error creation. Both (
3.66
) and Proposition
A.7
produce negligible nonlocal remainders which we notate with the superscript “
*
;” these terms obey extremely strong estimates (as in (
3.69
)) but do not obey spatial support properties (similar to (
2.28
)). The proof of Lemma
3.21
is contained in
[
25
, Lemma 10.6]
. The methodology is essentially identical to the pressure increments for the current errors, for example those constructed for the current oscillation error in subsection
5.2
. We refer also to the heuristics in subsection
3.4
, equation
3.90
.

Lemma 3.21

(
Current error from the velocity pressure increment).

There exists a current error
\phi_{\upsilon}
generated by
\sigma_{\upsilon}
such that the following hold.

(i)

We have the decomposition and equalities

\displaystyle\sigma_{\upsilon}=\sigma_{\upsilon}^{*}+\sum_{m^{\prime}=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{{q+\bar{n}}}\sigma_{\upsilon}^{m^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\phi_{{\upsilon}}=\underbrace{\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{*}}_{\textnormal{nonlocal}}+\underbrace{\sum_{m^{\prime}=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{{q+\bar{n}}}\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{m^{\prime}}}_{\textnormal{local}}

(3.67a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\phi_{\upsilon}(t,x)=D_{t,q}\sigma_{\upsilon}(t,x)

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\upsilon}}^{\prime}(t)\,.

(3.67b)

(ii)

For all
N\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
and
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1
and
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{m^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-100}(\pi_{q}^{m^{\prime}})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m^{\prime}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q}}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.68)

(iii)

For all
N\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
and
M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}+2}^{-10}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},{\tau_{q}^{-1}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.69)

(iv)

For all
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
,
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2\leq m\leq q+\bar{n}
, and
q+1\leq q^{\prime\prime}\leq m-1
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1})\cap B(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma^{2}_{q})=\emptyset\,,\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}(\phi_{{\upsilon}}^{m})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime\prime}}=\emptyset\,.

(3.70)

3.3.4 Velocity increment, new velocity field, and new velocity cutoffs

We can now define the velocity increment, velocity increment potential, and new velocity field.

Definition 3.22

(
Definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
,
\widehat{u}_{q+\bar{n}}=u_{q+1}
, and
\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n}}
).

Let
\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+\bar{n},x,t}
denote a space-time mollifier which is a product of compactly supported kernels at spatial scale
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
and temporal scale
\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}
. We assume that both kernels have vanishing moments up to
10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and are
C^{10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}
differentiable and define

\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}:=\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+\bar{n},x,t}w_{q+1}\,,\quad u_{q+1}=\widehat{u}_{{q+\bar{n}}}=u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}=\widehat{u}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\,,\quad\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n},k}:=\mathcal{\widetilde{P}}_{q+\bar{n},x,t}\upsilon_{q+1,k}\,.

(3.71)

The velocity increment and velocity increment potential satisfy the following pointwise estimates in terms of the velocity pressure increment
\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}
. These follow easily from standard mollification estimates and (
3.64b
), and we refer to
[
25
, Proposition 10.7]
for details.

Lemma 3.23

(
Pointwise estimates for
\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n}}
).

For
N+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
and
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{d}}
, we have that
\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n},k}
satisfies the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q+\bar{n}-1}D^{N}D_{t,q+\bar{n}-1}^{M}\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n},k}\right|

\displaystyle<{\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}}\left(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+2\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}{\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{i}\tau_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{2}}\right)\,.

(3.72)

With the velocity increment in hand, we can also define the new velocity cutoff functions. The construction of the velocity cutoffs is carried out in
[
25
, Section 9]
and is similar to the construction in
[
6
]
. These velocity cutoffs are defined inductively, which is motivated by the following analogy: propagation of material derivative estimates for
D_{t,q}=\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla
in
any
convex integration scheme should be done inductively by decomposing
D_{t,q}=\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla=D_{t,q-1}+\widehat{w}_{q}\cdot\nabla
. The main difference with
[
38
]
is that the Chebyshev inequality used to control the support of
\psi_{i,q}
utilizes inductive
L^{3}
bounds rather than inductive
L^{2}
bounds.

Proposition 3.24

(
New velocity cutoff functions).

There exists a set of new velocity cutoff functions
\psi_{i,q+\bar{n}}
such that all the listed inductive assumptions in subsections
2.3
hold.

Next, with the new velocity cutoffs in hand, we can verify nearly all of the inductive assumptions for the velocity increment
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
and velocity increment potential
\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n}}
.

Proposition 3.25

(
Inductive bounds for new velocity).

The velocity increment
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
, total velocity
\widehat{u}_{q+\bar{n}}
, and velocity increment potential
\widehat{\upsilon}_{q+\bar{n}}
satisfy (
2.9
) and all the inductive assumptions listed in subsection
2.6
, save for (
2.44
), which we verify by hand later in Lemma
6.8
.

Finally, we can dominate a weighted sum of the velocity cutoffs by the pressure increment
\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}
. This estimate is identical to
[
25
, Lemma 10.8]
. The proof of this estimate follows from the facts that the velocity pressure increment dominates
\nabla\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
from (
3.58
) and
\pi_{q}^{q+\bar{n}}
dominates
\nabla\widehat{u}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}
from (
2.29
) and (
2.38
), and so their sum dominates
\nabla\widehat{u}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
, which is itself comparable to the weighted sum of the velocity cutoffs on the left-hand side of (
3.73
). This result will be used in Lemma
6.8
.

Lemma 3.26

(
New inductive cutoffs are dominated by the pressure increment).

The new velocity cutoff functions
\psi_{i,q+\bar{n}}
satisfy

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{{i_{\rm max}}}\psi_{i,q+\bar{n}}^{2}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{2i}

\displaystyle\lesssim{r_{q}^{-2}}\left(\pi_{q}^{q+\bar{n}}+\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)\,.

(3.73)

3.4 Stress errors

Upon adding
\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
to (
2.7
), we must define a new stress error
S_{q+1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}(S_{q+1})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)w_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\cdot\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{TN}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1}\otimes w^{(p)}_{q+1}+R_{\ell}-\pi_{\ell}\mathrm{Id}\right)}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{O}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(w^{(p)}_{q+1}\otimes_{s}w^{(c)}_{q+1}+w^{(c)}_{q+1}\otimes w^{(c)}_{q+1}\right)}_{=:\,\mathrm{div}S_{C}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(R_{q}^{q}-R_{\ell}+\left(\pi_{\ell}-\pi_{q}^{q}\right)\mathrm{Id}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}S_{M1}}

(3.74)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1})+((\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1})\cdot\nabla)\widehat{u}_{q}+\mathrm{div}(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1})}_{=:\mathrm{div}S_{M2}}\,.

We have used the notations
R_{\ell}
and
\pi_{\ell}
to denote the mollifications of
R_{q}^{q}
and
\pi_{q}^{q}
; see Lemma
3.33
. Also,
u\otimes_{s}v=u\otimes v+v\otimes u
.
Note also that the transport and Nash error
S_{TN}
involves only
\widehat{u}_{q}
, but not
u_{q}
; this will be justified in Section
4
by showing that
w_{q+1}
has disjoint support from
u_{q}-\widehat{u}_{q}
. We then define the primitive stress error
\overline{R}_{q+1}=R_{q}-R_{q}^{q}+S_{q+1}
, so that
(u_{q+1},p_{q},\overline{R}_{q+1},-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}))
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{q+1}+\mathrm{div}\left(u_{q+1}\otimes u_{q+1}\right)+\nabla p_{q}=\mathrm{div}(-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}+\overline{R}_{q+1})\,,\qquad\mathrm{div}\,u_{q+1}=0\,.

(3.75)

Although a few of the terms in (
3.74
) are already in divergence form and sufficiently small, in general we must use Proposition
A.7
to produce each error term. The definition and estimation of the Reynolds stress error terms is contained in
[
25
, Section 8]
. We note first that the Reynolds stresses live at frequencies between (and including)
\lambda_{q+1}
and
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}
, which we denote with a superscript “
m
,” as in
S_{O}^{m}
for the oscillation stress error at frequency
\lambda_{m}
. Second, from (
A.27
), the inverse divergence first produces a spatially localized term, which we use the superscript “
l
” to indicate. Finally, the inverse divergence produces a nonlocal term from (
A.37
), which we indicate with a superscript “
*
;’’ as usual, the nonlocal terms are negligible and can be ignored. We now summarize the definitions.

Definition 3.27

(
\overline{R}_{q+1}
and
S_{q+1}^{m}
).

The transport-Nash error
S_{TN}
, oscillation error
S_{O}
, divergence corrector error
S_{C}
, and mollification error
S_{M}=S_{M1}+S_{M2}
can be decomposed into local and nonlocal portions at frequencies
\lambda_{q+1}
, …,
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}
as

S_{\bullet}=\sum_{m=q+1}^{{q+\bar{n}}}S_{\bullet}^{m}=\sum_{m=q+1}^{{q+\bar{n}}}S_{\bullet}^{m,l}+S_{\bullet}^{m,*}\,.

(3.76)

Then we define
S_{q+1}^{m}:=S^{m,l}_{q+1}+S^{m,*}_{q+1}
for all
q+1\leq m\leq q+\bar{n}
by

\displaystyle S^{m,l}_{q+1}

\displaystyle:=S^{m,l}_{O}+S^{m,l}_{TN}+S^{m,l}_{C}+S^{m,l}_{M}\,,\qquad S^{m,*}_{q+1}:=S^{m,*}_{O}+S^{m,*}_{TN}+S^{m,*}_{C}+S^{m,*}_{M}\,.

(3.77a)

Recalling (
2.10
) and (
2.28
), we then define the primitive
16
16

16

We refer to these stress errors as “primitive” since we will have to add a few small nonlocal pieces in (
6.37
). The local piece
R_{q+1}^{m,l}
requires no such adjustment, and so we do not notate it with a bar.
stress errors by

\displaystyle\overline{R}_{q+1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}\overline{R}_{q+1}^{m}\,,\qquad\overline{R}_{q+1}^{m}=R_{q}^{m}+S_{q+1}^{m}

(3.78)

\displaystyle R_{q+1}^{m,l}

\displaystyle:=R_{q}^{m,l}+S_{q+1}^{m,l}\,,\qquad\overline{R}_{q+1}^{m,*}:=R_{q}^{m,*}+S_{q+1}^{m,*}\,.

(3.79)

Next, associated to each
S_{q+1}^{m,l}
is a pressure increment
\sigma_{S^{m}}
, which is defined as in (
3.19
). We also define functions of time
\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{S^{m}}}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{S^{m}}}:=\int_{0}^{t}\langle D_{t,q}\sigma_{S^{m}}\rangle(s)ds\,,

(3.80)

which will be useful in (
3.91b
). The pressure increments dominate the local portions of the Reynolds stresses
S_{q+1}^{m,l}
for frequencies
m>q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
, while the local portions at any lower frequencies are actually dominated by anticipated pressure. We recall the basic estimates and support properties on stresses and their pressure increments in the following lemma, and refer to
[
25
, Section 8]
for details. The support properties are consequences of the results of Section
4
.

Lemma 3.28

(
Collected properties of stress error terms and pressure increments).

For each
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq q+\bar{n}
,
\sigma_{S^{m}}
satisfies the following properties.

(i)

For any
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{d}}
and
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S^{m,l}_{q+1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}+{\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}}\right)(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+18}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.81a)

(ii)

For
N,M\leq{\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{200}}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-9}\delta_{m+\bar{n}}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+18}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(3.82a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Gamma_{m}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+18}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(3.82b)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}+{\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}}\right)(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+18}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(3.82c)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{S^{m}}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-100}}\pi_{q}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+18}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.82d)

(iii)

\sigma_{S^{m}}
and
\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}
have the support properties

\displaystyle B(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\sigma_{S^{m}}=\emptyset\qquad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}\,,

(3.83a)

\displaystyle B(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}})\cap\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\sigma_{S^{m}}^{+}\cup\mathrm{supp\,}S^{m,l}_{q+1}\right)=\emptyset\qquad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1\,.

(3.83b)

(iv)

For
0\leq M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{S^{m}}}
defined in (
3.80
) satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{S^{m}}}\right|\lesssim(\max(1,T))^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.84)

With the previous lemma in hand, we may upgrade the material derivatives using Lemma
A.3
. We may also verify Hypothesis
2.8
. The proof of this lemma follows easily from Lemma
3.28
.

Lemma 3.29

(
Upgrading material derivatives and verifying Hypothesis
2.8
).

The new stress errors
S_{q+1}^{m}=S_{q+1}^{m,l}+S_{q+1}^{m,*}
satisfy the following.

(i)

R_{q+1}^{m,l}
satisfies Hypothesis
2.8
with
q
replaced by
q+1
.

(ii)

For
q+2\leq m\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
, the symmetric stresses
S_{q+1}^{m,l}
obey the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}S_{q+1}^{m,l}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma^{-50}_{m}\pi_{q}^{m}\Lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{m-1}^{i-5}\tau_{m-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(3.85)

for
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{10}}
. For the same range of
N,M
, the symmetric stress
S_{q+1}^{q+1,l}
obeys the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}S_{q+1}^{q+1,l}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma^{-50}_{{q+1}}\pi_{q}^{q+1}\Lambda_{{q+1}}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+19}\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.86)

(iii)

For
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq{q+\bar{n}}
and
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{100}}
, the symmetric stresses
S_{q+1}^{m,l}
obey the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,m-1}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}S^{m,l}_{q+1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sigma_{S_{O}^{m}}^{+}+\sigma_{S_{C}^{m,l}}^{+}+\mathbf{1}_{\{m={q+\bar{n}}\}}\left(\sigma_{S_{TN}}^{+}+\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}\right)+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{m-1}^{i-5}\tau_{m-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(3.87a)

(iv)

For all
q+1\leq m\leq{q+\bar{n}}
and
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the symmetric stresses
S_{q+1}^{m,*}

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}S^{m,*}_{q+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{2}\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{m-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.88)

Finally, the pressure increments
\sigma_{S^{m}}
produce current errors in exactly the same way the pressure increment
\sigma_{\upsilon}
for the velocity produced a current error in Lemma
3.21
. We refer to the discussion preceding Lemma
3.21
, as well as
[
25
, Section 8]
for further details. Let us pause, however, to explain why one may expect that these current errors do not require a new pressure increment, but are instead dominated by old intermittent pressure. Consider the portion of the highest shell of the oscillation error given by

\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}}\mathrm{div}\left(w_{q+1,R}\otimes w_{q+1,R}\right)\right)\approx\nabla R_{q}^{q}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}\,\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left(|\mathbb{W}_{q+1,R}|^{2}\right)\,,

where
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}
is a synthetic Littlewood-Paley projector defined in subsection
A.3
. In order to dominate the right-hand side pointwise, we recall (
2.26a
) and create a pressure increment
\sigma_{S_{O}^{q+\bar{n}}}
:

\sigma_{S_{O}^{q+\bar{n}}}=\lambda_{q}\pi_{q}^{q}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left|\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left(|\mathbb{W}_{q+1,R}|^{2}\right)\right|\,.

Considering the current error produced by the pressure increment in the highest shell
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}
only,
we now apply another synthetic Littlewood-Paley projector
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}
to the high frequency object, apply
D_{t,q}
, and invert the divergence. Imagining that
D_{t,q}
is analogous to multiplication by
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
, what we must estimate is then

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}\right|\lambda_{q}\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}\left|\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left(|\mathbb{W}_{q+1,R}|^{2}\right)\right|\right]\,.

(3.89)

Using (
2.29
) and (
2.38
), we may control
|\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}|
by
\lambda_{q}(\pi_{q}^{q})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}
. Next, we use (
3.8
) to control
\mathbb{W}_{q+1,R}
pointwise by
r_{q}^{-1}
, and we will gain two factors of
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
from the inverse divergences. Finally, recalling that
r_{q}^{-2}\approx\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\lambda_{q}^{-1}
,
\delta_{q}\approx\lambda_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}
, using the pressure scaling law (
2.25
), and writing everything out, we may bound (
3.89
) by

\lambda_{q}(\pi_{q}^{q})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q}\pi_{q}^{q}r_{q}^{-2}\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{-2}\leq(\pi_{q}^{q})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}r_{q}\leq(\pi_{q}^{q+\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\,.

(3.90)

Notice that this bound is
exactly
consistent with (
2.26b
), and so the current error coming from the addition of the new pressure increment is dominated by
anticipated
pressure and requires no new pressure increment! This avoids the possibility of a loop of pressure creation and new current error creation. The reader who absorbs these heuristics will be able to understand essentially every estimate in this paper related to the intermittent pressures
\pi_{q}^{q^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 3.30

(
Current error from the stress pressure increment).

For every
m\in\{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}\}
, the current error
\phi_{{S^{m}}}
satisfies the following properties.

(i)

We have the decompositions and equalities

\displaystyle\phi_{{S^{m}}}

\displaystyle=\phi_{{S^{m}}}^{*}+\sum_{m^{\prime}=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{{m}}\phi_{{S^{m}}}^{m^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\qquad\phi_{S^{m}}^{m^{\prime}}=\phi_{S^{m}}^{m^{\prime},l}+\phi_{S^{m}}^{m^{\prime},*}\,

(3.91a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\phi_{{S^{m}}}

\displaystyle=D_{t,q}\sigma_{{S^{m}}}-\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{S^{m}}}^{\prime}\,.

(3.91b)

(ii)

For
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m^{\prime}\leq m
and
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{S^{m}}^{m^{\prime},l}\right|<\Gamma_{m^{\prime}}^{-100}\left(\pi_{q}^{m^{\prime}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m^{\prime}}^{-1}(\lambda_{m^{\prime}}\Gamma_{m^{\prime}}^{2})^{M}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+17},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(3.92a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{S^{m}}^{m^{\prime},*}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{S^{m}}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}<\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}^{2})^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(3.92b)

(iii)

For all
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m^{\prime}\leq m
and all
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m^{\prime}-1
,

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\phi^{m^{\prime},l}_{S^{m}}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(3.93)

3.5 Transport and Nash current errors

In this section, we treat the transport and Nash current errors, which are defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\left(\overline{\phi}_{T}+\overline{\phi}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}|^{2}+\kappa_{q}^{q}-\frac{\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1})}{2}\right)

(3.94)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}):\left(w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}+R_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-\overline{R}_{q+1}\right)-\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{T}-\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{N}\,.

These error terms are in fact closely related to the new Reynolds stress error terms
S_{q+1}
, as one can see from (
3.94
). For this reason we have included the proofs of the estimates for these error terms in
[
25
, subsection 8.7]
. We however pause to discuss the heuristics behind these error terms, expanding upon the discussion in subsubsection
1.2.1
. First note that the cost of the material derivative operator in the first term is closely related to the size of
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
in the second term, and so we expect both terms to have the same size. Let us therefore consider the term

\left\|\mathrm{div}^{-1}\left[\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}\,:\,\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}}\left(w_{q+1,R}\otimes w_{q+1,R}\right)\right]\right\|_{1}\,.

From (
2.38
) and (
2.18
), we have that the
L^{3}
norm of
\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}
is essentially
\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q-\bar{n}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q}
. Next, we expect the inverse divergence to gain
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
, due to the presence of the synthetic Littlewood-Paley projector. Finally, recall that
w_{q+1,R}
uses
L^{2}
normalized pipes multiplied by coefficient functions
a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
, which from Lemma
3.16
have
L^{3}
norm
\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
(after aggregation). Combining these estimates and using that the high-frequency pipes decouple from the rest of the expression, we need that

\displaystyle\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q-\bar{n}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\delta_{{q+\bar{n}}}\leq\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}r_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\,.

We can draw an analogy with (
1.16
) by noticing that
\delta_{{q+\bar{n}}}
behaves essentially like
\delta_{q+1}
,
\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}
behaves essentially like
\delta_{q+2}
, and we have an extra
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
power of
r_{{q+\bar{n}}}
leftover, which behaves essentially like
r_{q+1}
. Then writing out everything in terms of
\lambda_{q}
as in (
1.16
), we see that this error term is sharp in
B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}_{3,\infty}
.

The following lemma contains the precise estimates satisfied by the transport and Nash current errors. We refer to
[
25
, subsection 8.7]
for details. As usual, though, the main properties are as follows: the error terms are contained in frequency shells between (and including)
\lambda_{q+1}
and
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}
; the shells at
\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}
and above require a pressure increment, while the lower shells are dominated by existing intermittent pressure; the pressure increments satisfy heuristic estimates exactly like those outlined in the above discussion; pressure increments and error terms themselves both satisfy support properties; and finally, nonlocal error terms are negligibly small.

Lemma 3.31

(
Current error and pressure increment from (
3.94
)).

There exist vector fields
\overline{\phi}_{TN}=\overline{\phi}_{T}+\overline{\phi}_{N}
and functions of time
\mathfrak{m}_{TN}=\mathfrak{m}_{T}+\mathfrak{m}_{N}
such that (
3.94
) holds. Furthermore, we may decompose
\overline{\phi}_{TN}
into components

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}_{TN}=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m}=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m,l}+\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m,*}

(3.95)

which satisfy the following.

(i)

The errors
\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m}
vanish uniformly for
q+2\leq m^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}-1
. Furthermore, the errors
\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}
for
m=q+1,q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
require no pressure increment. More precisely, we have that for
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{100}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}^{q+1,l}_{TN}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{-100}\left(\pi_{q}^{m}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}\lambda_{m}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\right)\,.

(3.96a)

(ii)

For
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq\bar{n}
, there exists functions
\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}=\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}-\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{-}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}^{m,l}_{{TN}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left((\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}+{\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}}\right)\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+17},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{9}}\right)

(3.97a)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{18}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{9}}\right)

(3.97b)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{m+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{m}^{-9}\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{18}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{9}}\right)

(3.97c)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{18}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{9}}\right)

(3.97d)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\lfloor\bar{n}/2\rfloor}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\pi_{q}^{q}\left(\lambda_{q+\lfloor\bar{n}/2\rfloor}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{18}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q}^{9}}\right)

(3.97e)

for all
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{100}}
.
Furthermore, we have that for
q+1\leq m^{\prime}\leq m-1
and
q+1\leq q^{\prime\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{-}\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime\prime}+1}\right)=\mathrm{supp\,}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{TN}}^{+}\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{m^{\prime}},\lambda_{m^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{m^{\prime}+1}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(3.98)

(iii)

When
m=q+2,\dots,q+\bar{n}
and
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1
, the local parts satisfy

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m,l}=\emptyset\,.

(3.99)

(iv)

For
m=q+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}-1
and
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}}{{4}}
, the non-local parts
{\overline{\phi}}^{m,*}_{O}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}{\overline{\phi}}^{m,*}_{{TN}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m-1})^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(3.100a)

For
q+\bar{n}
and
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}}{{4}}
, the non-local part
{\overline{\phi}}^{{q+\bar{n}},*}_{O}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{M}{\overline{\phi}}^{{q+\bar{n}},*}_{{TN}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}\right)\,.

(3.100b)

(v)

For
M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the time function
\mathfrak{m}_{TN}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{TN}\right|\lesssim\left(\max(1,T)\right)^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.101)

Just as for the premollified velocity increment and the new Reynolds stress errors, the pressure increment for the transport and Nash current errors produces a new current error, which is dominated by anticipated pressure. Applying the same heuristics as in (
3.90
) shows that the current error coming from the pressure increment for the transport and Nash current error is dominated by anticipated pressure. We refer to
[
25
, Lemma 8.21]
for details.

Lemma 3.32

(
Current error from the transport/Nash current error pressure increment).

For

every
m^{\prime}\in\{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}\}
, there exist current errors
\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}
associated to the pressure increments
\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}
and functions
\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}
of time
that satisfy the following properties.

(i)

We have the decompositions and equalities

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}+\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=D_{t,q}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}\,,

(3.102a)

\displaystyle\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}=\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}^{*}+\sum_{m=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{{m^{\prime}}}\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}^{m}\,,\qquad\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m}

\displaystyle=\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,l}+\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,*}\,.

(3.102b)

(ii)

For
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq m^{\prime}
and
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,l}\right|<\Gamma_{m}^{-100}\left(\pi_{q}^{m}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}^{2})^{M}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+{18}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,,

(3.103a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,*}\right\|_{\infty}<\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{m^{\prime}}\Gamma_{m^{\prime}}^{2})^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M},

(3.103b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}<\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{2})^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(3.103c)

(iii)

For all
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq m^{\prime}
and all
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1
,

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\phi^{m,l}_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(3.104)

(iv)

For
M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the mean part
\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{TN}^{m^{\prime}}}}\right|\leq(\max(1,T))^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3.105)

3.6 Mollification

In this section, we present two lemmas which estimate various mollification terms. These lemmas are essentially technical in nature and the reader can safely skip them. We refer to
[
25
, Lemmas 3.1, 8.22]
for details.

Lemma 3.33

(
Mollification and upgrading material derivative estimates).

Assume that
all
inductive assumptions listed in subsections
2.2
-
2.6
hold. Let
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}
be a space-time mollifier for which the kernel is a product of
\mathcal{P}_{q,x}(x)
, which is compactly supported in space at scale
\Lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{q,t}(t)
, which is compactly supported in time at scale
\mathrm{T}_{q-1}\Gamma_{q-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
; we further assume that both kernels have vanishing moments up to
10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
and are
C^{10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}
-differentiable. Define

\displaystyle R_{\ell}=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}R_{q}^{q}\,,\qquad\pi_{\ell}=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}\pi_{q}^{q}\,,\qquad\varphi_{\ell}=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}\varphi_{q}^{q}

(3.106)

on the space-time domain
[-\nicefrac{{\tau_{q-1}}}{{2}},T+\nicefrac{{\tau_{q-1}}}{{2}}]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
For
q^{\prime}
such that
q<q^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
, we define
\mathcal{P}_{q^{\prime},x,t}
in an analogous way after making the appropriate parameter substitutions, and we set
R_{\ell}^{q^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{q^{\prime},x,t}R_{q}^{q^{\prime}}
and
\pi_{\ell}^{q^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{q^{\prime},x,t}\pi_{q}^{q^{\prime}}
. For
q^{\prime}
with

Lemma 3.34

(
Mollification current errors).

There exist current errors
\overline{\phi}_{M}^{q+1}
and
\overline{\phi}_{M}^{q+\bar{n}}
and a function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{M}^{q+\bar{n}}
such that the following hold.

(i)

We have the equalities

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{M}^{q+1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div}\left(\varphi_{q}^{q}-\varphi_{\ell}\right)

(3.115a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{M}^{{q+\bar{n}}}+(\mathfrak{m}_{M}^{q+\bar{n}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}\left(|\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-|w_{q+1}^{2}|w_{q+1}\right)+(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1})\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}+(\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}):\right)\left(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}\right)\,.

(3.115b)

For all

, the mollification errors

and

satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}_{M}^{q+1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q+\bar{n}-1}^{M}\overline{\phi}_{M}^{q+\bar{n}}\right\|_{\infty}
