(3.117)

4
New velocity increment dodging

In our wavelet-inspired scheme scheme, the building block flows are the intermittent Mikado bundles presented in subsection
3.1
. The goal of this section is to specify the placement of these bundles. Specifically, we treat both the “bundling pipe”
\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\diamond}_{(\xi)}
and the highly intermittent pipe
\mathbb{W}^{I}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}=\mathrm{curl\,}\mathbb{U}^{I}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
appearing in (
3.47
). The placement of each intermittent bundle will be chosen to ensure disjointness from various other intermittent Mikado bundles. In subsection
4.1
, we consider a single prototypical term from (
3.47
) at the fixed time slice at which the flow map
\Phi_{(i,k)}
is the identity. This ensures that
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}
and
\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
are supported in perfectly straight, periodized cylinders. We then assume that the support of
a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
is inhabited by various
deformed
bundles and show that one can choose the supports of
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}
and
\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
to be disjoint from these bundles. We prove these placement lemmas in abstraction, so that in subsection
4.2
, we can apply them to choose the placements for each term in (
3.47
) inductively on the various indices present in the sum. It is in this subsection that we verify Hypotheses
2.5
–
2.6
.

4.1 Straight pipe dodging

Consider a rectangular prism
\Omega_{0}
, which in practice will be related to the support of the cutoffs
a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}
from a single term of (
3.47
). Assume that
\Omega_{0}
is inhabited by deformed pipes of thickness
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1},\dots\lambda_{q+\bar{n}-1}^{-1}
, corresponding to
\widehat{w}_{q+1},\dots,\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}
, and
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
, corresponding to terms from (
3.47
) whose placements have already been specified. If the prism
\Omega_{0}
is not too large, and the new bundle we are trying to place is sufficiently sparse, then we can place the new bundle to dodge the existing bundles. Furthermore, the pipes in the new bundle will be placed at distance no smaller than
\lambda_{q+i}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+i}
away from a given deformed pipe of thickness
\lambda_{q+i}^{-1}
. We call this additional property
effective dodging
. To see the importance of this property, consider the portion of the oscillation error given by

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+i}}\mathrm{div}(w_{q+1,R}\otimes w_{q+1,R})\,,

where
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+i}}
is a synthetic Littlewood-Paley projector defined in
A.3
. By the properties of this operator, the support of this error term will be contained in a
\lambda_{q+i}^{-1}
neighborhood of the support of
w_{q+1,R}
. By effective dodging, this error term will have spatial support which is disjoint from pipes of thickness
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1},\dots,\lambda_{q+i-1}^{-1}
. We will repeatedly use effective dodging in conjunction with the synthetic Littlewood-Paley projectors to ensure good spatial support properties of error terms and pressure increments.

Our first dodging proposition uses the bundling pipes to dodge pipes with thickness
at least

\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}
and
at most

\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
, corresponding to
\widehat{w}_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}
and
\widehat{w}_{q+1}
, respectively. We record and prove a statement for
\xi=e_{3}
and leave the case for general direction vectors to the reader. The argument proceeds by first isolating a single rectangular prism
\Omega_{0}
of dimensions
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}\times\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}\times\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
(corresponding to the support of a mildly anisotropic cutoff from Lemma
3.10
), and choosing the support of the bundling pipe to effectively dodge all given pipes of thicknesses
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1},\cdots\lambda_{q+\lfloor\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}\rfloor}^{-1}
in the prism.

Lemma 4.1

(
Using bundling pipes to dodge very old, thick pipes).

Let
\Omega_{0}
be a rectangular prism of dimensions
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}\times\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}\times\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
. Suppose that there exists a
q
-independent constant
\mathcal{C}_{P}
such that at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}
segments of their deformed segments (in the sense of Definition
4.4
) with thickness
\lambda_{q^{\prime}+\bar{n}}^{-1}
and spacing
({\lambda_{q^{\prime}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}})^{-1}
for some
q-\bar{n}<q^{\prime}\leq q-\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
have non-empty intersection with
\Omega_{0}
. Let
E_{0}\subset\Omega_{0}
denote the support of these deformed segments inside
\Omega_{0}
. Then for
\diamond=\varphi,R
, there exists
k\in\{1,\dots,\Gamma_{q}^{6}\}
and a bundling pipe flow
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{e_{3},k,\diamond}
defined as in Proposition
3.1
such that

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{e_{3},k,\diamond},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap E_{0}=\emptyset\quad\text{i.e.,}\quad B\left(E_{0},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\rho}}_{e_{3},k,\diamond}=\emptyset\,.

(4.1)

Proof.

We first divide the face
[0,\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}]^{2}
of the prism into the grid of squares of sidelength
\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}
, and
we will find a set of squares in which we can place a new bundling pipe flow
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{e_{3},k,\diamond}
. Since the set of squares will be placed
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{-4}}})^{2}
-periodically, we have from (
ii
) that

\displaystyle\text{(the possible number of placement of a set of squares)}=\left(\frac{\text{spacing}}{\text{thickness}}\right)^{2}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{4}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}}\right)^{2}=\Gamma_{q}^{6}\,.

By assumption there exist at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}
number of deformed pipe segments in the prism. When we enlarge these segments by a factor of
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}
and project the enlarged neighborhood onto the face
[0,\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}]^{2}
, each projection will be contained in a
\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}
-neighborhood of a curve of length at most
\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{5}
by (
4.5
) and (
4.6
). It then follows that
17
17

17

A fully rigorous version of this estimate would utilize a standard covering argument which is predicated on the geometric constraints imposed by Lemma
4.3
, or even Definition
4.4
; we however content ourselves with a slightly heuristic version and refer the reader to
[
6
, Proposition 4.8]
for further details.

(the number of grid squares occupied by given enlarged segments)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\textnormal{number of segments}\times\frac{\textnormal{area occupied by an enlarged segment}}{\textnormal{area of a grid square}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{P}\times\frac{\lambda_{q+1}^{-2}\Gamma_{q}^{7}}{\Gamma_{q}^{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q}^{5}\,,

which is less than
\Gamma_{q}^{6}
for sufficiently large
\lambda_{0}
. Therefore, from the pigeonhole principle, there exists a set of squares in which we can place the pipe
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{e_{3},k,\diamond}
satisfying (
4.1
).
∎

We now use the intermittent pipe flows from Propositions
3.3
or
3.4
to dodge pipes with thickness
at least

\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
and
at most
than
\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{-1}
, corresponding to
\widehat{w}_{{q+\bar{n}}}
and
\widehat{w}_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}
. This dodging is carried out on a prism of dimensions roughly
\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\times\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\times\lambda_{q}^{-1}
(corresponding to the support of a product of a mildly anisotropic cutoff from Lemma
3.10
and a strongly anistropic checkerboard cutoff from Lemma
3.11
). Combining this result with the previous lemma, we will have successfully dodged pipes of thicknesses in between
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
and
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
. As before, we present the statement for
\xi=e_{3}
and omit further details.

Lemma 4.2

(
Using very intermittent pipes to dodge newer, less thick pipes).

Let
\Omega_{1}
be a rectangular prism of dimensions
\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\times\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\times\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
with the long side in the
e_{3}
direction. Suppose that a finite number of sets of
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}}})^{3}
-periodic pipes of thickness
\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}
are given for all
q-\bar{n}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}<{q^{\prime\prime}}\leq q
, constructed as in Propositions
3.3
or
3.4
. Furthermore, suppose that for each such
{q^{\prime\prime}}
and any convex subset
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega_{1}
with
\textnormal{diam}\left(\Omega^{\prime}\right)\lesssim\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}^{-1}
, there exists a
q
-independent constant
\mathcal{C}_{P}
such that at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}
segments of the deformed pipes of thickness
\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}
have non-empty intersection with
\Omega^{\prime}
. For fixed
{q^{\prime\prime}}
, let
E_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}
denote the support of such segments inside
\Omega_{1}
. Then for either
\diamond=R
or
\diamond=\varphi
, there exists
k
and a corresponding intermittent pipe flow
\mathbb{W}_{e_{3},\diamond}:=\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+\bar{n}},\nicefrac{{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}}}{{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}}}
constructed as in Propositions
3.3
or
3.4
such that for all
q-\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}<{q^{\prime\prime}}\leq q
,

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\mathbb{W}_{e_{3},\diamond},\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{2}\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}\right)\cap E_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}=\emptyset\quad\text{i.e.,}\quad B\left(E_{{q^{\prime\prime}}},\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{2}\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\mathbb{W}_{e_{3},\diamond}=\emptyset\,.

Proof.

As in the previous lemma, since we want to place a new pipe which enjoys
effective dodging
with previously placed deformed pipes, instead of considering the previously placed pipes themselves, we consider a thickened neighborhood of them. More precisely, for a deformed pipe of thickness
2\lambda_{q+i}^{-1}
, we consider instead a neighborhood of it of thickness
\Gamma_{q+i}^{2}\lambda_{q+i}^{-1}
and call these new objects ‘thickened pipes’. Then, it is enough to place a new pipe that dodges these thickened pipes, so that a new pipe effectively dodges all previously placed deformed pipes.

We divide the face of
\Omega_{1}
into a grid of squares of sidelength
\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
. Since a new pipe will be placed
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}}})^{3}
-periodically, we have from Proposition
3.3
or
3.4
that

\displaystyle\text{(the possible number of placement of a set of squares)}=\left(\frac{\text{spacing}}{\text{thickness}}\right)^{2}=\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}}\right)^{2}\,.

(4.2)

Now, we count the number of grid squares occupied by given enlarged segments and compare it to this number. From the assumption that there exists
\mathcal{C}_{P}
which controls the density of thickened, deformed pipe segments of thickness
\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}
that can intersect a ball
\Omega^{\prime}
of volume
\approx(\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}})^{-3}
, we have that the total number of thickened pipe segments that can intersect
\Omega_{1}
is at most

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}\times\frac{\textnormal{length of $\Omega_{1}$}}{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}^{-1}}\times\frac{\textnormal{(width of $\Omega_{1}$})^{2}}{\min\left(\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}^{-1},\textnormal{width of $\Omega_{1}$}\right)^{2}}\leq\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}\times\frac{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}^{3}}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}}\,.

When we project all these thickened segments onto the face of
\Omega_{1}
, each projection will be contained in a
\approx\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{2}
-neighborhood of a curve of length at most
\approx\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}
from (
4.5
) and (
4.6
). Therefore, the number of grid squares occupied by each enlarged pipe projection is

\frac{\textnormal{area occupied by an enlarged segment}}{\textnormal{area of a grid square}}\approx\frac{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-1}\Gamma^{2}_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}}{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-2}}\,.

Thus the total number of grid squares covered by the union of all projections is

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{{q^{\prime\prime}}=q-\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{q}\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}\times\frac{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}}^{3}}{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}^{-{2}}}\frac{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{2}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}}{\Gamma_{q}^{8}\lambda_{q}}\,,

(4.3)

or the product of the two numbers computed above and summed over
{q^{\prime\prime}}
. This number will be less than the the number in (
4.2
) if

\displaystyle\bar{n}\mathcal{C}_{p}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{2}\Gamma_{q}^{-2}\frac{\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}{\lambda_{q}\lambda_{{q^{\prime\prime}}+\bar{n}}}\leq 1

for
q-\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq{q^{\prime\prime}}\leq q
, which is precisely (
7.1a
).
∎

4.2 Dodging for new velocity increment

Before getting to the main result of this subsection in Lemma
4.5
, we set up a few preliminaries and notations. First, recall that in the previous subsection, we ignored the fact that the objects in (
3.47
) contain flow maps
\Phi_{(i,k)}
which depend on time. In order to control the geometry of pipes which are deformed by these flow maps on a velocity field on a local Lipschitz timescale, we recall
[
38
, Lemma 3.7]
.

Lemma 4.3

(
Control on Axes, Support, and Spacing).

Consider a convex neighborhood of space
\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
. Let
v
be an incompressible velocity field, and define the flow
X(x,t)
and inverse
\Phi(x,t)=X^{-1}(x,t)
, which solves
\partial_{t}\Phi+v\cdot\nabla\Phi=0
with
\Phi|_{t=t_{0}}=x
. Define
\Omega(t):=\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}:\Phi(x,t)\in\Omega\}=X(\Omega,t)
. For an arbitrary
C>0
, let
\tau>0
be a timescale parameter and
\Gamma>3
a large multiplicative prefactor such that the vector field
v
satisfies the Lipschitz bound
\sup_{t\in[t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau]}\left\|\nabla v(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(t))}\lesssim\tau^{-1}\Gamma^{-2}
. Let
\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a set of straight pipe flows constructed as in Proposition
3.3
and Proposition
3.4

which are
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda r}})^{3}
-periodic and concentrated in the
\pi(4\lambda n_{*})^{-1}
-neighborhoods of axes
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
oriented in the vector direction
\xi
for
\xi\in\Xi,\Xi^{\prime}
Then
\mathcal{W}:=\mathcal{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda,r}(\Phi(x,t)):\Omega(t)\times[t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau]
satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

We have the inequality

\textnormal{diam}(\Omega(t))\leq\left(1+\Gamma^{-1}\right)\textnormal{diam}(\Omega)\,.

(4.4)

(2)

Let
x
and
y
belong to
A_{i}\cap\Omega
for some
i
, where the axes
A_{i}
are defined above. Denote the length of the axis
A_{i}(t):=X(A_{i}\cap\Omega,t)
in between
X(x,t)
and
X(y,t)
by
L(x,y,t)
. Then

L(x,y,t)\leq\left(1+\Gamma^{-1}\right)\left|x-y\right|\,.

(4.5)

(3)

The support of
\mathcal{W}
is contained in a
\displaystyle\left(1+\Gamma^{-1}\right)2\pi(4n_{\ast}\lambda)^{-1}
-neighborhood of the set

\bigcup_{i}A_{i}(t)\,.

(4.6)

(4)

\mathcal{W}
is “approximately periodic” in the sense that for distinct axes
A_{i},A_{j}
with
i\neq j
, we have

\left(1-\Gamma^{-1}\right)\mathrm{dist\,}(A_{i}\cap\Omega,A_{j}\cap\Omega)\leq\mathrm{dist\,}\left(A_{i}(t),A_{j}(t)\right)\leq\left(1+\Gamma^{-1}\right)\mathrm{dist\,}(A_{i}\cap\Omega,A_{j}\cap\Omega)\,.

(4.7)

A consequence of Lemma
4.3
is that a set of
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda r}})^{3}
-periodic intermittent pipe flows which are flowed by a locally Lipschitz vector field on the Lipschitz timescale can be decomposed into “segments of deformed pipe.” Furthermore, any neighborhood of diameter
\approx(\lambda r)^{-1}
contains at most a finite number of such segments of deformed pipe.

Definition 4.4

(
Segments of deformed pipes).

A single “segment of deformed pipe with thickness
\lambda^{-1}
and spacing
(\lambda r)^{-1}
” is defined as a
3\lambda^{-1}
neighborhood of a Lipschitz curve of length at most
2(\lambda r)^{-1}
.

We now recall and set a few notations which will be used in Lemma
4.5
. We first recall from (
2.6
) the notations
B(\Omega,\lambda^{-1})
and
B(\Omega,\lambda^{-1},\tau)
for space and space-time balls, respectively, around a space-time set
\Omega
. Using these notations and the definition of
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
from Definiton
3.22
, we have that

\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\subseteq B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{q+1},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\,.

(4.8)

Next, we recall the formula in (
3.6
) for an intermittent Mikado flow and the notation in (
3.2
) for a intermittent bundle, and we set

\displaystyle\varrho_{(\xi),\diamond}^{I}:=\xi\cdot\mathbb{W}_{(\xi),\diamond}^{I}\,.

(4.9)

Next, in slight conflict with (
2.6
), we shall also use the notation

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I},\lambda^{-1}\right):=\left\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\,:\,\exists y\in\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}\,,|x-y|\leq\lambda^{-1}\right\}

(4.10)

throughout this section, despite the fact that
\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
is not a set in space-time, but merely a set in space. We shall also use the same notation but with
\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
replaced by
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}
. Finally, for any smooth set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{3}
and any flow map
\Phi
defined in Definition
3.7
, we use the notation

\Omega\circ\Phi:=\left\{(y,t):t\in\mathbb{R},\Phi(y,t)\in\Omega\right\}=\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\circ\Phi\right)\,.

(4.11)

With this definition,
\Omega\circ\Phi
is the smooth space-time set whose characteristic function is annihilated by
D_{t,q}
.

We are now ready to verify the main dodging lemma for
w_{q+1}
, which as noted in (
4.8
) implies similar dodging properties for
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
.

Lemma 4.5

(
Dodging and preventing self-intersections for
w_{q+1}
and
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
).

We construct
w_{q+1}
so that the following hold.

(i)

Let
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
and fix indices
\diamond,i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l}
, which we abbreviate by
({(\xi)},\diamond)
, for a coefficient function
a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
(c.f. (
3.48
), (
3.49
)). Then

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{2}{\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}},{2\mathrm{T}_{q}}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)}}^{\diamond}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(4.12)

(ii)

Let
q^{\prime}
satisfy
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
, fix indices
({(\xi)},\diamond,I)
, and assume that
\Phi_{(i,k)}
is the identity at time
t_{{(\xi)}}
, cf. Definition
3.7
. Then we have that

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\zeta_{q,\diamond,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I,\diamond}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

\displaystyle\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\varrho^{I}_{(\xi),\diamond},\frac{1}{2}{\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}=\emptyset\,.

(4.13)

As a consequence we have

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\frac{1}{4}{\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}w_{q+1}=\emptyset\,.

(4.14)

(iii)

Consider the set of indices
\{({(\xi)},\diamond,I)\}
, whose elements we use to index the correctors constructed in (
3.47
), and let
\texttt{l},\overline{\texttt{l}}\in\{p,c\}
denote either principal or divergence corrector parts. Then if
(\overline{\diamond},(\overline{\xi}),\overline{I})\neq(\diamond,(\xi),I)
, we have that for any
\texttt{l},\overline{\texttt{l}}
,

\mathrm{supp\,}w_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{(\texttt{l}),I}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}w_{(\overline{\xi}),\overline{\diamond}}^{(\overline{\texttt{l}}),\overline{I}}=\emptyset\,.

(4.15)

(iv)

\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
satisfies Hypothesis
2.6
with
q
replaced by
q+1
.

Remark 4.6

(
Verifying Hypothesis
2.5
).

We claim that (
4.14
) and (
4.8
) imply that Hypothesis
2.5
holds with
q+1
replacing all instances of
q
. To check this, we must show that (
2.30
) holds for
q^{\prime},q^{\prime\prime}\leq{q+\bar{n}}
and
0<|q^{\prime}-q^{\prime\prime}|\leq\bar{n}-1
. By induction on
q
and the symmetry of
q^{\prime\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
, the only case we must check is the case that
q+\bar{n}=q^{\prime\prime}
and
0<{q+\bar{n}}-q^{\prime}\leq\bar{n}-1
. But it is a simple exercise in set theory to check that for
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq{q+\bar{n}}-1
, (
4.14
) is equivalent to
\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}}\cap B(\mathrm{supp\,}w_{q+1},\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2},2\mathrm{T}_{q})=\emptyset
. Then using (
4.8
) and the inequalities
\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}\geq\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}
,
b<2\implies\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\ll\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}^{2}
implies that (
2.30
) holds.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

We split the proof up into steps, in which we first carry out some preliminary set-up before verifying item (
i
), items (
ii
)–(
iii
), and finally item (
iv
).

Step 0: Ordering of cutoff functions and set-up.
Consider all coefficient functions
a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},\diamond}
utilized at stage
{q+1}
, cf. (
3.49
) and (
3.48
). Using natural numbers
z\in\mathbb{N}
as indices, we choose an ordering of the tuples
(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond)
such that for any choice of
(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond)
and
({i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*},\diamond^{*})
, we have

i<{i^{*}}\implies(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\diamond)<_{\textnormal{ordering}}({i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},\vec{l}^{*},\diamond^{*})\,,

(4.16)

where the implied inequality holds for the natural numbers assigned to each tuple in our chosen ordering. This automatically provides an ordering for the coefficient functions
a_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},\diamond}
and associated pipe bundles
\mathbb{B}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
. We will place pipe bundles inductively according to this ordering so that all the conclusions in the statement of Lemma
4.5
hold. (
4.16
) ensures that timescales are decreasing with respect to this ordering and mitigates the fact that the number of possible overlaps between
\psi_{i,q}\chi_{i,k,q}
and
\psi_{i+1,q}\chi_{i+1,k^{\prime},q}
could be of order
\Gamma_{q}
(see
3.14a
).
18
18

18

This is not strictly necessary– one can always adjust the choice of parameters to accommodate a spare
\Gamma_{q}
.
To lighten the notation, we will abbreviate the newly ordered coefficient and cutoff functions and associated intermittent pipe bundles (cf. (
3.48
), (
3.49
), and (
3.2
)) as

a_{z}\,,\psi_{z}\,,\omega_{z}\,,\chi_{z}\,,\zeta_{z}\,,\qquad\quad(\mathbb{B}\circ\Phi)_{z}=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{z}\mathbb{W}_{z}^{I})\circ\Phi_{z}\,,

respectively, where
z\in\mathbb{N}
corresponds to the ordering. Now for fixed
z
and
a_{z}
, we will place
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}
and
\mathbb{W}_{z}^{I}
with two goals in mind. First, we must dodge the velocity increments
\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}}
for
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
and
\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime\prime}}
for
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq q^{\prime\prime}\leq q+\bar{n}-1
. Second, we must dodge all pipe bundles
(\mathbb{B}\circ\Phi)_{\widehat{z}}
with coefficient functions
a_{\widehat{z}}
such that
\widehat{z}<z
in the aforementioned ordering.

Step 1: Proof of item (
i
).
We will apply Lemma
4.1
with the following choices. We recall that at the time
t_{z}
at which
\Phi_{z}
is the identity, the cutoff function
\eta_{z}
contains a checkerboard cutoff function
\zeta_{z}
which from Lemma
3.10
, item (
i
) is contained in a rectangular prism of dimensions no larger than
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
in the direction of
\xi_{z}
, and
\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\Gamma_{q}^{5}(\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}
in the directions perpendicular to
\xi_{z}
. Thus we set

\Omega_{0}=\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{z}\cap\{t=t_{z}\}\,.

Notice that
\textnormal{diam}(\Omega_{0})\leq\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-8}
, which satisfies (
2.31
) for
\bar{q}^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}
chosen as
\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}=q
and
\bar{q}^{\prime}=q^{\prime}
as in (
i
) so that
q+1\leq q^{\prime}=\bar{q}^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
. Then by applying Hypothesis
2.6
at level
q
with
\bar{q}^{\prime}=q^{\prime}
,
\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}=q
,
\Omega=\Omega_{0}
as defined above,
t_{0}=t_{z}
, and
\Phi_{\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}=\Phi_{z}
, we have that for each
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}
, there exists a set
L(q^{\prime},q,\Omega_{0},t_{z})
such that (
2.32
) and (
2.33
) hold. Now we set

E_{0}:=\bigcup_{q^{\prime}=q+1}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}L(q^{\prime},q,\Omega_{0},t_{z})\cap\{t=t_{z}\}\,,\qquad\mathcal{C}_{P}=\mathcal{C}_{D}\bar{n}\,.

We now appeal to the conclusion of Lemma
4.1
to choose a placement for
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}
such that

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap E_{0}=\emptyset\,.

(4.17)

An immediate consequence of (
4.17
), Hypothesis
2.6
, and (
3.16
) is that for
t
such that
|t-t_{z}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}
,

D_{t,q}\left(\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{z}}\mathbf{1}_{L(q^{\prime},q,\Omega_{0},t_{z})}\right)(t,x)\equiv 0\,

(4.18)

in distribution sense.
19
19

19

Here, we used that for any subset
A\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
,

\iint\mathbf{1}_{A\circ\Phi}D_{t,q}\phi\,dxdt=\iint\mathbf{1}_{A}\circ\Phi D_{t,q}\phi\,dxdt=\lim_{k\to\infty}\iint\eta_{k}\circ\Phi D_{t,q}\phi\,dxdt=0

holds for any
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R})
, where
\eta_{k}
is a sequence of smooth functions converging to
\mathbf{1}_{A}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
.
This in turn implies that in the same range of
t
,

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\circ\Phi_{z}(t)\cap L(q^{\prime},q,\Omega_{0},t_{z})\cap\{t=t_{z}\}=\emptyset\,.

(4.19)

Next, we claim that (
4.19
) implies that

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{t\}),\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap L(q^{\prime},q,\Omega_{0},t_{z})=\emptyset\quad\textnormal{for $|t-t_{z}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}$}\,,

(4.20)

which we now prove. Indeed, this follows from the fact that on the Lipschitz timescale
\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}
, spatial distances can change by at most a multiplicative factor of
(1\pm\Gamma_{q}^{-1})
due to (
2.38
) (see also Lemma
4.3
, which contains similar assertions). Finally, we claim that

\displaystyle B\left(\bigcup_{|t-t_{z}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}}\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{t\}),\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\subseteq\bigcup_{|t-t_{z}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}}B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{t\}),\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\,.

(4.21)

Assuming that (
4.21
) holds, we have then from (
4.20
), (
3.15
), and Hypothesis
2.6
that

B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\widetilde{\chi}_{z}\zeta_{z}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\right),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2},2\mathrm{T}_{q}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}}=\emptyset\,,

which is equivalent to (
4.12
) after using the same sort of set-theoretic reasoning as in Remark
4.6
. To prove (
4.21
), suppose that
(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{t})
belongs to the set on the left-hand side of the inclusion in (
4.21
). Then by definition, there exists
(t_{0},x_{0})
such that
|\widetilde{t}-t_{0}|\leq 2\mathrm{T}_{q}
,
|\widetilde{x}-x_{0}|\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}
,
|t_{0}-t_{z}|\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}
, and
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{t_{0}\})
. Then from (
2.15
) and (
7.5
), we have that
|\widetilde{t}-t_{z}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2}
. So we need to find
x^{\prime}
such that
(x^{\prime},\widetilde{t})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{\widetilde{t}\})
and
|x^{\prime}-\widetilde{x}|<\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}
. Now from (
3.16
), (
2.39b
), Corollary
3.8
, and (
7.5
), we have that

\displaystyle\left\|{\Phi_{z}}(\widetilde{t},\cdot)-\Phi_{z}(t_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{T}_{q}\left\|\partial_{t}\Phi_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{z}-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2},t_{z}+\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2})\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{T}_{q}\left\|\widehat{u}_{q}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times(t_{z}-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2},t_{z}+\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}+2})\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\,.

Therefore, although it may not be the case that
(x_{0},\widetilde{t})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{\widetilde{t}\})
, there must exist
x^{\prime}
such that
|x^{\prime}-x_{0}|\leq\Gamma_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
and
(x^{\prime},\widetilde{t})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{\widetilde{t}\})
.
20
20

20

We have that
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}\circ\Phi_{z}
if and only if
\Phi_{z}(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}
. Using the bound on the difference between
\Phi_{z}(\widetilde{t})
and
\Phi_{z}(t_{0})
, we may say that although
\Phi_{z}(x_{0},\widetilde{t})
is not necessarily in the support of
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{z}
, it is very close.
Since
|x^{\prime}-\widetilde{x}|\leq|x^{\prime}-x_{0}|+|x_{0}-\widetilde{x}|\leq\Gamma_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}<\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}
, we have thus concluded the proof of (
4.21
).

Step 2: Proofs of items (
ii
) and (
iii
).
In order to proceed with this portion of the proof, we assume inductively that a version of Hypothesis
2.6
holds for the portion of
w_{q+1}
already constructed. More precisely, we extend the ordering on
z\in\mathbb{N}
from
Step 0
to ordered pairs
(z,I)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
such that
\widehat{z}<z\implies(\widehat{z},\widehat{I})<(z,I)
for any
\widehat{I},I
(that is, we fix
z
and finish placing an entire bundle for all its various values of
I
before moving to different
\widehat{z}
). We thus assume the following inductive hypothesis.

Hypothesis 4.7

(
Density of already placed pipe bundles in
w_{q+1}
).

There exists a geometric constant
\mathcal{C}_{\rm pipe}
such that the following holds. Fix
z
and set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{\widehat{z},\widehat{I}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{supp\,}\left[\chi_{\widehat{i},\widehat{k},q}\zeta_{(\widehat{\xi})}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}^{\diamond}_{(\widehat{\xi})}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\widehat{I},\diamond}_{\widehat{\xi}}\right)\circ\Phi_{(\widehat{i},\widehat{k})}\right]\cap B\left(\varrho^{\widehat{I}}_{(\widehat{\xi}),\diamond},\frac{1}{2}{\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}}\right)\circ\Phi_{(\widehat{i},\widehat{k})}\cap(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\{t\})\,,

\displaystyle w_{z,I}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{(\widehat{z},\widehat{I})<(z,I)}{a_{\widehat{z}}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\widehat{z}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\widehat{z}}^{\widehat{I}}\mathbb{W}^{\widehat{I}}_{\widehat{z}})\circ\Phi^{\widehat{I}}_{\widehat{z}}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{S}_{z,I}(t):=\bigcup_{(\widehat{z},\widehat{I})<(z,I)}\mathfrak{s}_{\widehat{z},\widehat{I}}(t)\,.

Let
i_{z}
be the value of
i
corresponding to
z
and
a_{z}
, and let
t_{0}
be any time and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
be a convex set with diameter at most
\left(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{-1}
such that
\Omega\times\{t_{0}\}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i_{z},q}\neq\emptyset
. Let
\Phi
solve

with initial data

. We set

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\Omega,z,I}=\#\left\{(\widehat{z},\widehat{I})<(z,I)\,:\,\exists t\in[t_{0}-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}-2},t_{0}+\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{-i_{z}-2}]\textnormal{ with }\mathfrak{s}_{\widehat{z},\widehat{I}}(t)\cap\Omega(t)\neq\emptyset\right\}\,.

\displaystyle\left[\mathrm{supp\,}w_{z,I}(\cdot,t)\cap\Omega(t)\right]\subseteq\left[\mathfrak{S}_{z,I}(t)\cap\Omega(t)\right]\subseteq\left[\Phi(t)^{-1}(L_{(z,I)})\cap\Omega(t)\right]\,.

(4.22)

One should understand this hypothesis as asserting that at all steps in the construction of

,

We will now justify the application of Lemma

5
Error estimates for the relaxed local energy inequality

In this section, we estimate the main error terms in the relaxed local energy inequality. In subsection
5.1
, we carefully identify each error term. Then in subsections
5.2
–
5.5
, we estimate the oscillation current error, linear current error, stress current error, and divergence corrector current errors, respectively. Finally, we summarize the results and upgrade the material derivatives in subsection
5.6
.

5.1 Defining new current error terms

We will define
\overline{\phi}_{q+1}
by adding
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
to
u_{q}
on the left-hand side of (
2.8
) and collecting new errors generated by the addition. Recall that in (
3.75
) we added
\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
to the Euler-Reynolds system and obtained the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{q+1}+\mathrm{div}\left(u_{q+1}\otimes u_{q+1}\right)+\nabla p_{q}=\mathrm{div}\left(\overline{R}_{q+1}-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}\right)

(5.1)

for
u_{q+1}=u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}
, where

\displaystyle\overline{R}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=R_{q}-R_{q}^{q}+S_{q+1}

(5.2a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}S_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\nabla u_{q}+\mathrm{div}\left(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+R_{q}^{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}\right)\,.

(5.2b)

We recall that
\kappa_{q}=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mathrm{tr\,}(R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id})
,
\kappa_{q}^{q}=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\mathrm{tr\,}(R_{q}^{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})
and set
22
22

22

We are using the definition of
S_{M1}
from (
3.74
) to achieve the third equality.

\displaystyle\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}{\mathrm{tr\,}(\overline{R}_{q+1}-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id})}=\kappa_{q}-\kappa_{q}^{q}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1})=\kappa_{q}-\kappa_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1}-S_{M1})\,,

(5.3a)

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{q}-\varphi_{q}^{q}+\overline{\phi}_{q+1}\,,

(5.3b)

where
\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{q+1}
will include the new errors. We now introduce a function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}(t)
to account for the fact that the new errors may not have zero mean for each time and expect to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q+1}|^{2}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q+1}|^{2}+p_{q}\right)u_{q+1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla){\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}}+\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q+1}\right)+\mathrm{div}\overline{\varphi}_{q+1}+\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}-E\,.

(5.4)

Towards this end, we first note that since
\mathrm{div}\,\widehat{u}_{q}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\left((R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q}\right)+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\mathrm{div}\left(\overline{R}_{q+1}-R_{q}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q}\right)+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:\left(R_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-\overline{R}_{q+1}\right)\,.

(5.5)

We now add and subtract
\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q+1}\right)
in the second to last equality below to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}+p_{q}\right)(u_{q}+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}})\right)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{ineq:relaxed.LEI}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\kappa_{q}+\mathrm{div}((R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q})+\mathrm{div}\varphi_{q}+(\partial_{t}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)+\nabla u_{q}:\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+u_{q}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\mathrm{div}(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}})\right)-E

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:dodging:oldies}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\kappa_{q}+\mathrm{div}((R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q})+\mathrm{div}\varphi_{q}+(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\mathrm{div}(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}})\right)-E

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{eq:ovR:def:redux},\\
\eqref{ER:new:error:redux}\end{subarray}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}+\kappa_{q}\right)+\mathrm{div}((R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q})+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\varphi_{q}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\mathrm{div}(\overline{R}_{q+1}-R_{q}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})-E

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:desert:helpfull}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}+\kappa_{q}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\varphi_{q}\right)+\mathrm{div}((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q+1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})-\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})(\widehat{u}_{q+1}-\widehat{u}_{q})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+R_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-\overline{R}_{q+1})-E

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla){\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}}+\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})\widehat{u}_{q+1}\right)+\mathrm{div}\overline{\varphi}_{q+1}+\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}-E\,.

(5.6)

We see that the final quantity on the right-hand side of (
5.6
) will hold provided that

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}_{q+1}+{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:def:ov:phi}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}+\kappa_{q}\right)-(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})(\widehat{u}_{q+1}-\widehat{u}_{q})\right)+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})

\displaystyle\qquad+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+R_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-\overline{R}_{q+1})+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\varphi_{q}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:def:ov:kappa}}{=}(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}+\kappa_{q}^{q}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1})\right)+(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}-(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathrm{div}\left((\overline{R}_{q+1}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}+\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id})(\widehat{u}_{q+1}-\widehat{u}_{q})\right)+\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})

\displaystyle\qquad+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+R_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}-\overline{R}_{q+1})+\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}+\varphi_{q}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq:def:ov:kappa}}{=}\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}|^{2}+{\kappa_{q}^{q}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1})\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{T}+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\mathrm{div}((\widehat{u}_{q+1}-\widehat{u}_{q})\overline{\kappa}_{q+1})-\mathrm{div}\left(\left(\overline{R}_{q+1}-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id}\right)(\widehat{u}_{q+1}-\widehat{u}_{q})\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{R}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{w_{q+1}\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{L}+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{L}}+\underbrace{\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}+R_{q}{-\pi_{q}^{q}\mathrm{Id}}-\overline{R}_{q+1})}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{N}+\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{N}}\,,

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}^{(p)}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(p)}+\varphi_{\ell}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{O}}+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}^{(c)}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(p)}+(w_{q+1}^{(c)}\cdot w_{q+1}^{(p)})w_{q+1}^{(p)}+\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(c)}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{C}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathrm{div}(\varphi_{q}^{q}-\varphi_{\ell})+(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\frac{1}{2}\left(|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}-|w_{q+1}|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}\left(|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-|w_{q+1}|^{2}w_{q+1}\right)}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{M1}+\mathfrak{m}_{M1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1})\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})+\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1})}_{=:\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{M2}+\mathfrak{m}_{M2}^{\prime}}\,,

where
\mathfrak{m}_{T},\mathfrak{m}_{N},\mathfrak{m}_{L},\mathfrak{m}_{M1},\mathfrak{m}_{M2}
are functions of time only and are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{T}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\left(\frac{1}{2}|w_{q+1}|^{2}+\kappa_{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr\,}(S_{q+1})\right)\right\rangle(s)\,ds

(5.7a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{N}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1}+R_{q}-R_{q+1})\right\rangle(s)\,ds

(5.7b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{L}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle w_{q+1}\cdot(\partial_{t}\widehat{u}_{q}+(\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\widehat{u}_{q}+\nabla p_{q})\right\rangle(s)\,ds

(5.7c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{M1}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\frac{1}{2}\left(|\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}|^{2}-|w_{q+1}|^{2}\right)\right\rangle(s)\,ds

(5.7d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{M2}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1})\cdot(\partial_{t}u_{q}+(u_{q}\cdot\nabla)u_{q}+\nabla p_{q})\right\rangle(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\nabla\widehat{u}_{q}:(\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}\otimes\widehat{w}_{q+\bar{n}}-w_{q+1}\otimes w_{q+1})\right\rangle(s)\,ds\,.

(5.7e)

The notation
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes the spatial average of a tensor, and we define
\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}:=\mathfrak{m}_{T}+\mathfrak{m}_{N}+\mathfrak{m}_{L}+\mathfrak{m}_{M1}+\mathfrak{m}_{M2}
.

With these definitions in hand, we can rewrite (
5.6
)
for
(u_{q+1},p_{q},\overline{R}_{q+1},\overline{\varphi}_{q+1},-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q}))

as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q+1}|^{2}\right)+\mathrm{div}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|u_{q+1}|^{2}+p_{q}\right)u_{q+1}\right)

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}+\widehat{u}_{q+1}\cdot\nabla)(\overline{\kappa}_{q+1}+{\mathfrak{m}_{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}}})+\mathrm{div}\left({(\overline{R}_{q+1}-(\pi_{q}-\pi_{q}^{q})\mathrm{Id})}\widehat{u}_{q+1}\right)+\mathrm{div}\overline{\varphi}_{q+1}-E\,.

(5.8)

The primitive current error
\overline{\phi}_{q+1}
will consist of
\overline{\phi}_{q+1}^{k}
, so that
\overline{\phi}_{q+1}=\sum_{q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}{\overline{\phi}_{q+1}^{k}}
.

5.2 Oscillation current error

Recalling the definition of
\mathbb{B}_{(\xi),\varphi}
and
\mathbb{B}_{(\xi),R}
from (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{B}_{a}\mathbb{B}_{b}\mathbb{B}_{b})_{(\xi),\varphi}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{3}\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{6}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left[(\mathbb{W}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{I})_{a}(\mathbb{W}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{I})_{b}(\mathbb{W}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{I})_{b}\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{3}\xi_{a}\xi_{b}\xi_{b}+\xi_{a}\xi_{b}\xi_{b}

\displaystyle(\mathbb{B}_{a}\mathbb{B}_{b}\mathbb{B}_{b})_{(\xi),R}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi),R}^{3}\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{9}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left[(\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},R}^{I})_{a}(\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},R}^{I})_{b}(\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},R}^{I})_{b}\right]

where we used
\langle|\mathbb{W}^{I}_{{(\xi)},\varphi}|^{2}\mathbb{W}^{I}_{{(\xi)},\varphi}\rangle=|\xi|^{2}\xi
from Proposition
3.4
item (
5
),

\langle\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi),\varphi}^{3}\rangle=1
from Proposition
3.1
item (
iii
),
\sum_{I}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{6}=1
from (
3.4
), and
\langle|\mathbb{W}^{I}_{{(\xi)},R}|^{2}\mathbb{W}^{I}_{{(\xi)},R}\rangle=0
from Proposition
3.3
item (
5
). Using that all cross-terms from
|w_{q+1}^{(p)}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(p)}
(as defined in (
3.46
)) vanish due to Lemma
4.5
item (
iii
), and the fact that
(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi)\cdot\nabla
gives zero when applied to
(\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\left(|w_{q+1}^{(p)}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(p)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}\left[\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\left|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right|^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div}\left(a_{(\xi),\varphi}^{3}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi\right)+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}b_{(\xi),\varphi}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\varphi}^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}b_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})\,,

(5.9)

where the
\gamma
component of
b_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
is given by
b_{(\xi),\diamond}^{\gamma}=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\xi_{a}\partial_{\gamma}\left(a_{(\xi),\diamond}^{3}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{a}^{\gamma}\right)
,
\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}
is the pipe density defined in (
3.2
), and we are using the notation
3\diamond
in the power of
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I}
as a stand-in for
6
or
9
in the current and Reynolds cases, respectively.

By the choice of
a_{(\xi),\varphi}
, the first term cancels out
\varphi_{\ell}
up to a higher-frequency error term. Indeed, using (
3.48
), (
3.50
), (
3.27c
) to yield a formula for the summation of
\zeta_{q,\varphi,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}^{3}
, (
3.28
), (
2.13
) at level
q
, (
3.14d
) at level
q
, and (
3.20
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}

\displaystyle a_{(\xi),\varphi}^{3}|\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\xi|^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{a}^{\gamma}\xi_{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}\psi_{i,q}^{6}\omega_{j,q}^{6}\chi_{i,k,q}^{6}\zeta_{q,\varphi,i,k,\xi,\vec{l}}^{3}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\xi}^{3}\left(\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}}\right)(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{a}^{\gamma}\xi_{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,l^{\perp}}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}\psi_{i,q}^{6}\omega_{j,q}^{6}\chi_{i,k,q}^{6}\mathcal{X}_{q,\xi,l^{\perp}}^{3}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\xi}^{3}\left(\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}}\right)(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{a}^{\gamma}\xi_{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k}\psi_{i,q}^{6}\omega_{j,q}^{6}\chi_{i,k,q}^{6}{c_{3}}\left(-{\frac{1}{c_{3}}\varphi^{\gamma}_{\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,l^{\perp}}\underbrace{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}\psi_{i,q}^{6}\omega_{j,q}^{6}\chi_{i,k,q}^{6}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\xi}^{3}\left(\frac{\varphi_{q,i,k}}{\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{3(j-1)}_{q}}\right)(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{a}^{\gamma}\xi_{a}}_{=:\,2\widetilde{b}^{\gamma}_{(\xi)}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\mathcal{X}^{3}_{q,\xi,l^{\perp}}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=-(\varphi_{\ell})^{\gamma}+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,l^{\perp}}\widetilde{b}^{\gamma}_{(\xi)}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\mathcal{X}^{3}_{q,\xi,l^{\perp}}\right)\left(\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\,.

(5.10)

The inverse divergence of the remaining terms will form new current errors. We first recall the synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition (cf. Section
A.3
). Since
\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I}
is defined on the plane
\xi^{\perp}
and is periodized to scale
\left(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}r_{q}\right)^{-1}{=(\lambda_{q+\lfloor\bar{n}/2\rfloor}\Gamma_{q})^{-1}}
from (
3.2
), and Propositions
3.3
,
3.4
, we can decompose
\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}
in front of
(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}
into

\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{q+\lfloor n/2\rfloor{+1}}}^{\xi}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}+{\sum_{m={q+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+{2}}}^{q+\bar{n}+1}}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(m-1,m]}^{\xi}+(1-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{q+\bar{n}+1}^{\xi})\,.

Assuming we can apply the inverse divergence operator from Proposition
A.7
(with the adjustments set out Remark
A.10
for pointwise bounds), we define

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}^{q+1}_{O}

\displaystyle:=(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\underbrace{b_{(\xi),\varphi}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\varphi}^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})}_{=:\,t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},\varphi}^{q+1}}

(5.11a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,l^{\perp}}\underbrace{\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{b}^{\gamma}_{(\xi)}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\mathcal{X}^{3}_{q,\xi,l^{\perp}}\right)\left(\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)}_{=:\,\widetilde{t}^{q+1}_{i,j,k,\xi,l^{\perp},\varphi}}

(5.11b)

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}^{q+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}_{O}

\displaystyle:=(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\underbrace{b_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{\xi}_{q+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})}_{=:\,t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}}

(5.11c)

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}

\displaystyle:=(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\underbrace{b_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{\xi}_{(m-1,m]}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})}_{=:\,t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m}}

(5.11d)

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}^{q+\bar{n}}_{O}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m=q+\bar{n}}^{q+\bar{n}+1}(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\underbrace{b_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{\xi}_{(m-1,m]}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})}_{=\,t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m}}

(5.11e)

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\left[\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l},I,\diamond}b_{(\xi),\diamond}\left(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{3}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})^{3\diamond}(1-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{q+\bar{n}+1}^{\xi})\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(\varrho_{{(\xi)},\diamond}^{I})^{3}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})\right]\,,

(5.11f)

where (
5.11d
) is defined for
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1<m<q+\bar{n}
. We justify these applications and record estimates on the outputs in the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.1

(
Current error and pressure increment).

There exist current errors
\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m}
for
m=q+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}
and pressure increments
\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m}}^{+}=\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m}}-\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m}}^{-}
for
m=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}
such that the following hold.

(i)

We have the equality

\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}\left(|w_{q+1}^{(p)}|^{2}w_{q+1}^{(p)}+\varphi_{\ell}\right)=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m}=\sum_{m=q+1}^{q+\bar{n}}\mathrm{div}\left(\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m,l}+\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m,*}\right)\,.

(ii)

The lowest shell has no pressure increment; more precisely,
\sigma^{+}_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{q+1}}\equiv 0
, and for
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{100}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}^{q+1}_{O}\right|

\displaystyle<\Gamma_{q}^{65}\Lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}(\pi_{q}^{q})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\Gamma_{q}^{8}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(5.12)

(iii)

For all
m=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\overline{\phi}^{m,l}_{O}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left((\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{+})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\right)\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.13a)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{+}+\delta^{2}_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.13b)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{+}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle<\delta_{m+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{m}^{-9}\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.13c)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{+}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\Gamma_{m}^{\mathsf{C_{\infty}}-9}\left(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.13d)

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m}_{O}}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle<\left(\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\lfloor\bar{n}/2\rfloor}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\pi_{q}^{q}\left(\lambda_{q+\lfloor\bar{n}/2\rfloor}\Gamma_{q}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.13e)

for all
N,M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{100}}
. Furthermore, we have that for all
m^{\prime}\geq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1
,

\begin{split}B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\overline{\phi}^{m,l}_{O}&=\emptyset\qquad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1\\
B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m^{\prime}}_{O}}^{+})&=\emptyset\qquad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m^{\prime}-1\\
B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\overline{\phi}^{m^{\prime}}_{O}}^{-})&=\emptyset\qquad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}\,.\end{split}

(5.14)

(iv)

For all
m=q+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}
and
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
, the non-local part
\bar{\phi}^{m,*}_{O}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\bar{\phi}^{m,*}_{O}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\lambda_{m}^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(5.15)

Proof.

The equality in (
i
) follows from (
5.9
)–(
5.11
), assuming for the moment that all quantities in (
5.11
) are well-defined. We now split the proof up into cases. We first treat
\overline{\phi}_{O}^{q+1}
as defined in (
5.11a
)–(
5.11b
) and prove (
5.12
) and (

) for

. Next, we treat (

) and prove (

)–(

) for

using Proposition

in conjunction with Remark

. Afterwards we treat the intermediate shells from (

) and a portion of the last shell (

), and prove (

)–(

) for

using Proposition

. Finally, we treat (

), which will be absorbed to the nonlocal part of the current error. We will therefore prove (

) using Proposition
A.7
. We fix the following choices throughout the proof,

\displaystyle v=\widehat{u}_{q}\,,\quad\Phi=\Phi_{(i,k)}\,,\quad\mathcal{C}_{v}=\Lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,,\quad\nu^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{8}\,,\quad\lambda^{\prime}=\Gamma_{q}^{13}\Lambda_{q}\,,\quad

\displaystyle 2M_{*}=N_{*}=\frac{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}{3}\,,\quad M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}\,,\quad M_{\circ}=N_{\circ}=2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}\,,\qquad K_{\circ}\textnormal{ as in \eqref{i:par:9.5}/\eqref{ineq:K_0}}

while the remaining parameters will vary depending on the case.

Lemma 5.2

(
Pressure current).

For every
m^{\prime}\in\{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}\}
, there exist a current error
\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}
associated to the pressure increment
\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}
defined by Lemma
5.1
and a function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}}
which satisfy the following properties.

(i)

We have the decompositions and equalities

\displaystyle\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}^{*}+\sum_{m=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}^{{m^{\prime}}}\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}^{m},\quad\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m}=\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,l}+\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,*}\,

(5.38a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}}}^{\prime}\,=D_{t,q}\sigma_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}\,.

(5.38b)

(ii)

For
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq m^{\prime}
and
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,l}\right|<\Gamma_{m}^{-100}\left(\pi_{q}^{m}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{m}^{-1}(\lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}^{2})^{M}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+17},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.39a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{m,*}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}<\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{m^{\prime}}\Gamma_{m^{\prime}}^{2})^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(5.39b)

(iii)

For all
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1\leq m\leq m^{\prime}
and all
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq m-1
,

\displaystyle B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\Gamma_{q^{\prime}+1}\right)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\phi^{m,l}_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(5.40)

(iv)

The function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}}}

satisfies that for
M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}(t)=\int_{0}^{T}\left\langle D_{t,q}\sigma_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}\right\rangle(s)\,ds\,,\quad\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{\sigma_{\phi_{{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}}}}\right|\leq(\max(1,T))^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(5.41)

Proof.

We follow the case numbering from Lemma
5.1
. Note that the only cases which require a pressure increments are

and

)–(

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}=\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}^{*}+\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}^{0}+\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}^{1}\,.

and define the current error

\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}:=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}
which has a decomposition into

\iota=0,1\end{subarray}}\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}^{\iota}

(5.42a)

\displaystyle\leq{\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+60}}{\pi_{q}^{q}}{\Lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}}{\left(\frac{r_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}{r_{q}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\Gamma_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(5.43)

\textnormal{of low-freq. coeff's}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\Lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}}_{\textnormal{$\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}$ power of freq. gain}}\underbrace{\Gamma_{q}^{51}\left(\frac{r_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}}{r_{q}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}}_{\textnormal{lower order}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\Gamma_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}}_{\textnormal{intermittency loss}}\underbrace{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}^{-1}}_{\textnormal{inv. div. gain}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+15},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Gamma_{q}^{-150}r_{q}^{-1}\left(\pi_{q}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}\Gamma_{q}^{2})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+16},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)

(5.44)

for all

.

Case 3:
In this case we consider the higher shells from the oscillation error. The general principle is that the estimate will only be sharp in the
m=m^{\prime}={q+\bar{n}}
double endpoint case, for which the intermittency loss is most severe. We now explain why this is the case by parsing estimates (
5.43
) and (
5.44
). We incur a material derivative cost of
\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+60}
, which is converted into
r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q}(\pi_{q}^{q})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
using (
2.29
) and the rough definition of
\tau_{q}^{-1}=\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}r_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
, or equivalently Corollary
3.15
. The rescaled size of the high-frequency coefficients from the oscillation error is always
1
(see the choices of
\mathcal{C}_{*,1}
from the last lemma), and remains so upon being raised to the
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
power in (
A.55
). The low-frequency coefficient function from a trilinear oscillation error incurs a derivative cost of
\lambda_{q}
(which we have grouped with “frequency gain") and is dominated by
(\pi_{\ell})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}
, at which point the
r_{q}^{-1}
is scaled out due to the
L^{1}-L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
scaling balance between current and stress errors (see (
A.57
), (
A.60
)). The negative power in the frequency gain is determined by which shell of the oscillation error is being considered. The lower order terms may essentially be ignored. Next, we have an
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\rightarrow L^{\infty}
intermittency loss, which is used to pointwise dominate the high-frequency portion of the pressure increment using the
L^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
norm and prevent a loop of new current error and new pressure creation. Finally, we have an inverse divergence gain depending on which synthetic Littlewood-Paley shell of the pressure increment we are considering. The net effect is that the “frequency gain" upgrades the
\pi_{\ell}
to
\pi_{q}^{m}
since
m\leq m^{\prime}
, the half power of
\pi_{q}^{q}
is upgraded using
\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
from the cost of
D_{t,q}
and
\lambda_{m}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}}
from the inverse divergence gain, and the remaining
\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\lambda_{m}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}
is strong enough to absorb the intermittency loss, with a perfect balance in the case

m=m^{\prime}={q+\bar{n}}\qquad\implies\qquad\left(\frac{\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\lambda_{{q+\bar{n}}}^{-\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\approx 1\,.

In order to fill in the details, we now recall the choices of
\bar{m}
and
\mu_{m}
from (
5.31
). For the sake of brevity we ignore the slight variation in the case of the first projector for
m^{\prime}=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2
and focus on the second projector for
m^{\prime}=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2
and the other cases
q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2<m^{\prime}\leq{q+\bar{n}}
. We have from (
A.62
) that

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}=\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{*}+\sum_{\iota=0}^{{m^{\prime}-q-\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{\iota}\,.

and define the current error
\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}:=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}
which has a decomposition into

\displaystyle\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{*}\,,\qquad\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+1}=\sum_{{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{0}\,,

\displaystyle\phi_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond\\
\iota=1,2\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{\iota}\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\overline{\phi}_{O}^{m^{\prime}}}^{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond\\
\iota=m\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}t_{i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l},I,\diamond}^{m^{\prime}}}^{\iota}\quad\textnormal{if $q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m=q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+\iota{\leq}m^{\prime}$}\,.

As in the previous case, we make further decomposition into the local and nonlocal parts,

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+60}\pi_{q}^{q}\Lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\frac{r_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)}}{r_{q}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\lambda_{m^{\prime}-1}^{-2}\lambda_{m^{\prime}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\frac{\min(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)},\lambda_{q+\bar{n}})\Gamma_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)-1}^{-2}\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)}\left(\min(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)},\lambda_{m^{\prime}})\Gamma_{q}^{2}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i+14},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

Next, from (

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle\varpi

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{q}^{60}\pi_{q}^{q}\left(\frac{\Lambda_{q}\lambda_{m^{\prime}}}{\lambda_{m^{\prime}-1}^{2}}\cdot\frac{r_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)}}{r_{q}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\frac{\min(\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)},\lambda_{q+\bar{n}})\Gamma_{q}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\frac{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)}}{\lambda_{q+\nicefrac{{\bar{n}}}{{2}}+m(\iota)-1}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}a_{{(\xi)},\diamond}(\boldsymbol{\rho}_{(\xi)}^{\diamond}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\xi}^{I})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}}\,.

5.3 Linear current error

Lemma 5.3

(
Definition and basic estimates).

There exists a current error
\overline{\phi}_{L}=\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n}}
and a function of time
\mathfrak{m}_{L}
such that the following hold.

(i)

We have the equality and decomposition

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n}}+\mathfrak{m}_{L}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=w_{q+1}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)\,,\qquad\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n}}=\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n},l}+\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n},*}\,.

(5.46)

(ii)

For all
N+M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}}{{4}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q+\bar{n}-1}D^{N}D_{t,{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n},l}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{-100}\left(\left(\pi_{q}^{q+\bar{n}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}+\left(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}\right)^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\right)r_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q+\bar{n}-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{i{+4}},\mathrm{T}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{2}\right)

(5.47a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{M}\overline{\phi}_{L}^{q+\bar{n},*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau^{-1}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1},\mathrm{T}_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{{q+\bar{n}}-1}^{2}\right)\,.

(5.47b)

(iii)

For all
q+1\leq q^{\prime}\leq q+{q+\bar{n}}-1
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\phi_{L}^{q+\bar{n},l}\right)\cap B\left(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\Gamma_{q^{\prime}-1}\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}\right)=\emptyset\,.

(5.48)

(iv)

The time function
\mathfrak{m}_{L}
satisfies
\mathfrak{m}_{L}^{\prime}=\langle w_{q+1}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)\rangle
and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d^{M+1}}{dt^{M+1}}\mathfrak{m}_{L}\right|\leq(\max(1,T))^{-1}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\quad\text{for }M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}}}{{4}}\,.

(5.49)

Proof.

Step 0: Splitting the error term and upgrading material derivatives.
We use the Euler-Reynolds system (
2.7
), the mollified stresses and pressures from (
3.112
) and (
3.108
), respectively, and the formula for the premollified velocity increment potential from Remark
3.18
to split the error

\displaystyle w_{q+1}\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}u_{q}+u_{q}\cdot\nabla u_{q}+\nabla p_{q}\right)=w_{q+1}\cdot\mathrm{div}\left(R_{q}-\pi_{q}\mathrm{Id}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(w_{q+1}-e_{q+1})\cdot\mathrm{div}\left(\sum_{m=q}^{q+\bar{n}-1}R_{\ell}^{m}-\sum_{m=q}^{q+\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{pr}}-1}\pi_{\ell}^{m}\mathrm{Id}\right)}_{=\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{L1}^{q+\bar{n}}+\mathfrak{m}_{L1}^{\prime}}+\underbrace{e_{q+1}\cdot\mathrm{div}\left(\sum_{m=q}^{q+\bar{n}-1}R_{\ell}^{m}-\sum_{m=q}^{q+\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{pr}}-1}\pi_{\ell}^{m}\mathrm{Id}\right)}_{=\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{L2}^{q+\bar{n}}+\mathfrak{m}_{L2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{w_{q+1}\cdot\mathrm{div}\left(\sum_{m=q}^{q+\bar{n}-1}(R_{q}^{m}-R_{\ell}^{m})-\sum_{m=q}^{q+\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{pr}}-1}(\pi_{q}^{m}-\pi_{\ell}^{m})\mathrm{Id}\right)}_{=\mathrm{div}\overline{\phi}_{L3}^{q+\bar{n}}+\mathfrak{m}_{L3}^{\prime}}\,.

Notice that the tail of the sum
\sum_{q+\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{pr}}}^{\infty}\pi_{q}^{k}
of
\pi_{q}
does not appear because of (
2.24
). The term
\overline{\phi}_{L1}^{q+\bar{n}}
is the main term and requires sharp estimates from the inverse divergence operator from Lemma
A.12
, while
\overline{\phi}_{L2}^{q+\bar{n}}
and
\overline{\phi}_{L3}^{q+\bar{n}}
may be estimated much more brutally.

Step 1: Estimating the main local term.
In order to estimate
\overline{\phi}_{L1}^{q+\bar{n}}
, we need to upgrade the material derivative estimates on
w_{q+1}-e_{q+1}=\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{d}}\upsilon_{q+1}
. Towards this end, we claim that on
\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,m}
and for all
N\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}-2{\mathsf{d}}^{2}
,
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
, and
q+1\leq m\leq{q+\bar{n}}-1
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{{\mathsf{d}}-k}\left|D^{N}D_{t,m}^{M}\partial_{i_{1}}\cdots\partial_{i_{k}}\upsilon_{q+1}\right|\lesssim(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{m}^{-1}\Gamma_{m}^{i-5},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{9}\right)\,.

(5.50)

This estimate follows from dodging; more precisely, we appeal to (
iii
) and (
3.55
) from Lemma
3.17
,
and (
ii
) from Lemma
4.5

to assert that for all
m=q+1,\dots,q+\bar{n}-1
,
\widehat{w}_{m}\cdot\nabla\upsilon_{q+1}\equiv 0
. Then using (
3.58
) from Lemma
3.19
and applying (
2.13
) and (
2.19
) concludes the proof.

We now fix
i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
and
m=q+1,q+2,\dots,q+\bar{n}-1
(the cases
m=q
and
m\geq q+\bar{n}
will require minor modifications) and apply Lemma
A.12
with the following choices:

\displaystyle G=\mathrm{div}\left(R_{\ell}^{m}-\pi_{\ell}^{m}\mathrm{Id}\right)^{\bullet}\,,\quad\varrho=(w_{q+1}-e_{q+1})^{\bullet}\,,\quad\vartheta=\upsilon_{q+1}^{\bullet}\,,\quad v=\widehat{u}_{m-1}\,,\quad\lambda^{\prime}=\Lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}\,,

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{12}\,,\quad\nu=\tau_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+23}\,,\quad N_{*}=\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}-2{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\,,\quad M_{*}=\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{d}}\textnormal{ as in \eqref{i:par:10}/\eqref{ineq:dpot:1}}\,,

\displaystyle\pi^{\prime}=\left(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\,,\quad\Omega=\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,m-1}\,,\quad\pi=2\Gamma_{m}^{3}\pi_{\ell}^{m}\Lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}\,,\quad M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}\,,

\displaystyle\lambda=\Lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}\,,\quad\Upsilon=\Lambda=\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}\,,\quad M_{\circ}=N_{\circ}=3\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}\,,\quad K_{\circ}\textnormal{ as in \eqref{i:par:9.5}/\eqref{ineq:K_0}}\,.

Then we have that (
A.23b
) is satisfied by (
2.38
), (
A.44a
) is satisfied from (
3.108c
) and (
3.112
), (
A.44b
) is satisfied from (
3.58
) and (
5.50
), and (

) is satisfied by definition and by (

).

We then conclude from (
A.47
) that for all
N\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}-2{\mathsf{d}}^{2}-{\mathsf{d}}
and
M\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{5}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,m-1}}D^{N}D_{t,m-1}^{M}\mathcal{H}\left((w_{q+1}-e_{q+1})^{\bullet}\mathrm{div}\left(R_{\ell}^{m}-\pi_{\ell}^{m}\mathrm{Id}\right)^{\bullet}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Gamma_{m}^{3}\pi_{\ell}^{m}\Lambda_{m}\Gamma_{m}\left(\sigma_{\upsilon}^{+}+\delta_{q+2\bar{n}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}r_{q}^{-1}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}(\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{m-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{i+4},\mathrm{T}_{m}^{-1}\Gamma_{m-1}^{2}\right)\,.

(5.51)

From (
A.46
) and (
3.55
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\mathcal{H}\left((w_{q+1}-e_{q+1})^{\bullet}\mathrm{div}\left(R_{\ell}^{m}-\pi_{\ell}^{m}\mathrm{Id}\right)^{\bullet}\right)\right)\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\upsilon_{q+1}\,,

(5.52)

which leads to (

) from (

) and (

). Indeed, from (

) and Lemma
