]
.

A.1 Commutators and sums and products of operators

We recall
[
25
, Lemmas A.6, A.7]
; for the original versions we refer to
[
6
, Lemmas A.10, A.14]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty]
.
Fix
N_{x},N_{t},N_{*},M_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
, let
v
be a vector field, let
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
, and let
\Omega
be a space-time domain. Assume that the vector field
v
obeys

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}Dv\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{v}\mathcal{M}\left(N+1,N_{x},\lambda_{v},\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,N_{t},\mu_{v},\widetilde{\mu}_{v}\right)

(A.1)

for
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
.
Moreover, let
f
be a function which obeys

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}f\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{f}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda_{f},\widetilde{\lambda}_{f}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,N_{t},\mu_{f},\widetilde{\mu}_{f}\right)

(A.2)

for all
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
.
Denote

\displaystyle\lambda=\max\{\lambda_{f},\lambda_{v}\},\quad\widetilde{\lambda}=\max\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{f},\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}\},\quad\mu=\max\{\mu_{f},\mu_{v}\},\quad\widetilde{\mu}=\max\{\widetilde{\mu}_{f},\widetilde{\mu}_{v}\}.

Let
m,n,\ell\geq 0
be such that
n+\ell\leq N_{*}
and
m\leq M_{*}
.
Then the commutator
[D_{t}^{m},D^{n}]
is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|D^{\ell}\left[D_{t}^{m},D^{n}\right]f\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{f}\mathcal{M}\left(\ell+n,N_{x},\lambda,\widetilde{\lambda}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(m,N_{t},\max\{\mu,\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}\},\max\{\widetilde{\mu},\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}\}\right).

(A.3)

Lemma A.2
.

Fix
p\in[1,\infty]
,
N_{x},N_{t},N_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
, and a space-time domain
\Omega\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}
. Assume that for all
N,M\leq N_{*}
, the vector field
v
and function
f
obey (
A.1
) and (
A.2
). Define
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+(v\cdot\nabla)
. Next, let
w
be a vector field such that for
k\geq 1
and
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
with
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N_{*}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}D^{\alpha_{i}}{D_{t}^{\beta_{i}}}\right)w\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{w}\mathcal{M}\left(|\alpha|,N_{x},\lambda_{w},\widetilde{\lambda}_{w}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(|\beta|,N_{t},\mu_{w},\widetilde{\mu}_{w}\right)

(A.4)

for some
\mathcal{C}_{w}\geq 0
,
1\leq\lambda_{w}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{w}
, and
1\leq\mu_{w}\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{w}
.
Then, we have that for all
N+M\leq N_{*}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}(D_{t}+(w\cdot\nabla))^{M}f\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{f}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\lambda,\widetilde{\lambda}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,N_{t},\mu,\widetilde{\mu}\right)\,,

(A.5)

where

\displaystyle\lambda=\max\{\lambda_{f},\lambda_{v},\lambda_{w}\},\quad\widetilde{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\max\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{f},\widetilde{\lambda}_{v},\widetilde{\lambda}_{w}\},\quad\mu=\max\{\mu_{f},\mu_{v},\mu_{w},\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{v},\mathcal{C}_{w}\widetilde{\lambda}_{w}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}

\displaystyle=\max\{\widetilde{\mu}_{f},\widetilde{\mu}_{v},\widetilde{\mu}_{w},\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{v},\mathcal{C}_{w}\widetilde{\lambda}_{w}\}\,.

A.2 Upgrading material derivatives

We now recall
[
25
, Lemma A.23]
, which upgrades material derivatives for functions decomposed into a “localized” piece with support assumptions and a “nonlocal” remainder.

Lemma A.3

(
Upgrading material derivatives).

Fix
p\in[1,\infty]
and a positive integer
N_{\star}\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{4}}
. Assume that a tensor
F
is given with a decomposition
F=F^{l}+F^{*}
which satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}F^{l}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{p,F}\lambda_{F}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+c}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(A.6a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}F^{*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,F}\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{F}^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}

(A.6b)

for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
, an absolute constant
c\leq 20
, and constants
\mathcal{C}_{p,F}
and
\mathcal{C}_{*,F}
. Assume furthermore that there exists
k
such that
q+1<k\leq q+\bar{n}
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\widehat{w}_{q^{\prime}},\lambda_{q^{\prime}}^{-1}{\Gamma_{q^{\prime}}})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(F^{l})=\emptyset\quad\forall q+1\leq q^{\prime}<k\,.

(A.7)

Finally, assume that

\lambda_{F}\Gamma_{q+\bar{n}}^{{i_{\rm max}}+2}\delta_{q+\bar{n}}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{q}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\leq\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{-1}\,.

(A.8)

Then
F
obeys the following estimate with an upgraded material derivative for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
;

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,k-1}D^{N}D_{t,k-1}^{M}F\right\|_{p}\lesssim\ (\mathcal{C}_{p,F}+\mathcal{C}_{*,F})\max(\lambda_{F},\Lambda_{k-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{k-1}^{i}\tau_{k-1}^{-1},\Gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{k-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

(A.9)

In particular, the nonlocal part
F^{*}
obeys better estimate

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,k-1}^{M}F^{*}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,F}\max(\lambda_{F},\lambda_{k-1}\Gamma_{k-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{k-1}^{-1},\mathrm{T}_{k-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\right)

(A.10)

for
N+M\leq N_{\star}
. Similarly, if instead of (
A.6a
),
F^{l}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}F^{l}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi_{F}\lambda_{F}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+c}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(A.11)

for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
, an absolute constant
{c\leq 24}
, and a positive function
\pi_{F}
with
\pi_{F}\geq\mathcal{C}_{*,F}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{i,k-1}D^{N}D_{t,k-1}^{M}F\right|\lesssim\ \pi_{F}\max(\lambda_{F},\Lambda_{k-1})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{k-1}^{i}\tau_{k-1}^{-1},\Gamma_{k-1}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{k-1}^{-1}\right)

(A.12)

for all
M+N\leq N_{\star}
, provided that (
A.8
) holds.

A.3 Synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition

A synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition replaces the convolution kernel from the standard Littlewood-Paley decomposition with a compactly supported kernel, so that the spatial support of the output is controlled. We then replace the standard Littlewood-Paley decomposition of a function into frequency shells with a decomposition using so-called synthetic Littlewood-Paley projectors, notated by

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}(\rho)+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(\lambda_{k-1},\lambda_{k}]}(\rho)\right)+\left(\mathrm{Id}-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{K}}\right)(\rho)\,.

(A.13)

As we recall in this section, each synthetic projector above obeys similar derivative estimates as the standard frequency projectors, but has controlled spatial support. We recall from
[
25
, Section 4.3]
the definition and give two lemmas showing that effective “inverse divergences” can be defined for functions to which the synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition has been applied.

Definition A.4

(
Synthetic Littlewood-Paley projector).

Let
\bar{\varphi}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\nicefrac{{-1}}{{\sqrt{2}}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{2}}})
have unit mass
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\varphi}ds=1
and have vanishing moments
\int_{\mathbb{R}}s^{n}\bar{\varphi}ds=0
for
n=1,\dots,10\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
. Define
\bar{\varphi}_{\lambda}(\cdot)=\lambda\bar{\varphi}(\lambda\cdot)
and set
\varphi_{\lambda}(x)=\bar{\varphi}_{\lambda}(x_{1})\bar{\varphi}_{\lambda}(x_{2})
. For
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
(which we identify with a periodic function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
), we define the
synthetic Littlewood-Paley projectors
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda}f(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\lambda}(y)f(x-y)dy\,,\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]}f:=(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{2}}-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{1}})f\,.

(A.14)

Lemma A.5

(
Inverse divergence with synthetic LP projectors: lowest and highest shells).

Fix

q\in[1,\infty]
. Let
\mathsf{N}
a positive integer,
N_{**}\leq\nicefrac{{\mathsf{N}}}{{2}}
a positive integer,
r,\lambda
such that
\lambda r,\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\rho:(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda r}})^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
a smooth function such that there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}
with

\left\|D^{N}\rho\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\lambda^{N}\,.

(A.15)

for
N\leq\mathsf{N}
. Let
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{K}
be given with
\lambda r<\lambda_{0}<\lambda<\lambda_{K}
. If the kernel
\overline{\varphi}
used in Definition
A.4
has
N_{**}
vanishing moments, then for
p\in[q,\infty]
we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}\rho\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda r}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{q}}-\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}}\lambda_{0}^{N}\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall N\leq\mathsf{N}\,,

(A.16a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\left(\left(\mathrm{Id}-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{K}}\right)\rho\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{K}}\right)^{N_{**}}\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\lambda^{N+3}\qquad\forall N\leq\mathsf{N}-N_{**}-3\,.

(A.16b)

Furthermore, for any chosen positive even integer
{\mathsf{D}}
and any small positive number
\alpha
, there exist rank-
{\mathsf{D}}
tensor potentials
\vartheta_{0}
and
\vartheta_{K}
such that for
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{D}}
and
N
in the same range as above,

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{D}}\vartheta_{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\rho\,,\qquad\qquad\left\|D^{N}\mathrm{div}^{k}\vartheta_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{\alpha}\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda r}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{q}}-\nicefrac{{2}}{{p}}}(\lambda r)^{k-{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,{\mathsf{D}}-k,\lambda r,\lambda_{0}\right)\,,

(A.17a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}^{\mathsf{D}}\vartheta_{K}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}-\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\lambda_{K}})\rho\,,\qquad\left\|D^{N}\mathrm{div}^{k}\vartheta_{K}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{K}}\right)^{N_{**}}\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\lambda^{3}(\lambda r)^{k-{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,{\mathsf{D}}-k,\lambda r,\lambda\right)\,.

(A.17b)

The implicit constants above depend on
\alpha
but do not depend on
\lambda
,
\lambda_{0}
,
\lambda_{K}
, or
r
.

Lemma A.6

(
Inverse divergence with synthetic LP projectors: intermediate shells).

Fix

q\in[1,\infty]
. Let
\rho:\mathbb{T}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function which is
\left(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda r}}\right)^{2}
-periodic and for
N\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
satisfies

\left\|D^{N}\rho\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\lambda^{N}\,.

(A.18)

Then for any
\lambda r<\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}
and a given
{\mathsf{D}}\geq 1
, there exists a tensor field

\Theta_{\rho}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{(2^{\mathsf{D}})}
such that for
p\in[q,\infty]
,
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{D}}
,
0<\alpha\ll 1
, and
N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}\mathrm{div}\right)^{({\mathsf{D}})}\Theta_{\rho}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]}(\rho)=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]}(\rho-\langle\rho\rangle)

(A.19a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\partial_{i_{1}\cdots i_{{\mathsf{D}}-k}}(\lambda_{1}^{-{\mathsf{D}}}\Theta_{\rho}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}})^{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{\mathsf{D}})}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{{\mathsf{D}},\alpha}\mathcal{C}_{\rho,q}\left(\frac{\min\left(\lambda,\lambda_{2}\right)}{\lambda r}\right)^{\frac{2}{q}-\frac{2}{p}+\alpha}\lambda_{1}^{-k}\min\left(\lambda,\lambda_{2}\right)^{N}\,,

(A.19b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\Theta_{\rho}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}})

\displaystyle\subset B(\mathrm{supp\,}(\rho),\lambda_{1}^{-1})\,.

(A.19c)

The implicit constants above depend on
\alpha
but do not depend on
\lambda
,
\lambda_{1}
,
\lambda_{2}
, or
r
.

A.4 Inversion of the divergence

Our general strategy for solving the divergence equation
\mathrm{div}\varphi=g-\langle g\rangle
follows the approach of
[
6
, Propositions A.17, A.18]
. Specifically, we consider
g=G\varrho\circ\Phi
, where
G
has a significantly lower effective frequency than the minimum frequency of
\varrho
. The solution includes a local part
\mathcal{H}g
, and a nonlocal part
\mathcal{R}^{*}g
that is negligible in size. For the proofs we refer the reader to
[
6
, Propositions A.17, A.18]
or
[
25
, Section A.3]
.

Proposition A.7

(
Main inverse divergence operator).

Let
n\geq 2
and
p\in[1,\infty]
be free parameters.

Part 1: Low-frequency assumptions

(i)

Let
G
be a vector field and assume there exist a constant
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}>0
and parameters

N_{*}\geq M_{*}\geq 1\,,

(A.20)

M_{t}
, and
\lambda,\nu,\nu^{\prime}\geq 1
such that for all
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,.

(A.21)

(ii)

Fix an incompressible vector field
v(t,x):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and denote its material derivative by
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
. Let
\Phi
be a volume preserving diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{T}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle D_{t}\Phi=0\,\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\left\|\nabla\Phi-\mathrm{Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\,.

(A.22)

Denote by
\Phi^{-1}
the inverse of the flow
\Phi
, which is the identity at a time slice which intersects the support of
G
.
Assume that the velocity field
v
and the flow functions
\Phi
and
\Phi^{-1}
satisfy the bounds

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}+\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\prime N}

(A.23a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}Dv\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu\lambda^{\prime N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,,

(A.23b)

for all
N\leq N_{*}
,
M\leq M_{*}
, and some
\lambda^{\prime}>0
.

Part 2: High-frequency assumptions

(i)

Let
\varrho\colon\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be a zero mean scalar function such that there exists a large positive even integer
{\mathsf{d}}\gg 1
and a smooth, mean-zero, tensor potential
24
24

24

We use
i_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq{\mathsf{d}}
to denote any number in the set
\{1,\dots,n\}
.

\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}:\mathbb{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\left(n^{\mathsf{d}}\right)}
such that
\varrho(x)=\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{\mathsf{d}}}\vartheta^{(i_{1}\dots i_{\mathsf{d}})}(x)
.

(ii)

There exists a parameter
\mu\geq 1
such that
\varrho
and
\vartheta
are
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\mu}})^{n}
-periodic.

(iii)

There exist parameters
1\ll\Upsilon\leq\Upsilon^{\prime}\leq\Lambda
,
\mathcal{C}_{*,p}>0
such that for all
0\leq N\leq{N_{*}}
and all
0\leq k\leq{\mathsf{d}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{k}}\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,p}\Upsilon^{k-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,{\mathsf{d}}-k,\Upsilon^{\prime},\Lambda\right)\,.

(A.24)

(iv)

There exists
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
such that the above parameters satisfy

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime},\lambda\ll\mu\leq\Upsilon\leq\Upsilon^{\prime}\leq\Lambda\,,\qquad\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Upsilon^{-2}\Upsilon^{\prime}\leq 1\,,\qquad N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}\geq 2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+n+1\,,

(A.25)

where by in the first inequality in (
A.25
) we mean that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{n+1}\left(\frac{\mu}{2\pi\sqrt{3}\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})}\right)^{-{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}\leq 1\,.

(A.26)

Part 3: Localized output

(i)

There exists a vector field
R=:\mathcal{H}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)
and scalar-valued function
E
such that

\displaystyle G\;\varrho\circ\Phi

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div}R+E=:\mathrm{div}\left(\mathcal{H}\left(G\varrho\circ\Phi\right)\right)+E\,.

(A.27)

(ii)

The support of
R
is a subset of
\mathrm{supp\,}G\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\vartheta
.

(iii)

There exists an explicitly computable positive integer
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{H}}
, an explicitly computable function
r(j):\{0,1,\dots,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{H}}\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and explicitly computable tensors

\displaystyle\rho^{\beta(j)}\,,\qquad\beta(j)=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{r(j)})\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{r(j)}\,,

\displaystyle H^{\alpha(j)}\,,\qquad\alpha(j)=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{r(j)},k,\ell)\in\{1,\dots,n\}^{r(j)+1}\,

of rank
r(j)
and
r(j)+1
, respectively, all of which depend only on
G
,
\varrho
,
\Phi
,
n
,
{\mathsf{d}}
, such that the following holds. The error
R
can be decomposed into a sum of localized errors as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{k}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)=R^{k}=\sum_{j=0}^{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{H}}}H^{\alpha(j)}\rho^{\beta(j)}\circ\Phi\,,

(A.28)

where the contraction is on the first
r(j)
indices. Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}H^{\alpha(j)}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}G\,,\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}\rho^{\beta(j)}\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}\vartheta\,.

(A.29)

(iv)

For all
N\leq N_{*}-\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}
,
M\leq M_{*}
, and
j\leq\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{H}}
, we have the subsidiary estimates

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\rho^{\beta{(j)}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,p}\Upsilon^{-2}\Upsilon^{\prime}\mathcal{M}\left(N,1,\Upsilon^{\prime},\Lambda\right)

(A.30a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}H^{\alpha{(j)}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\left(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,.

(A.30b)

(v)

For all
N\leq N_{*}-\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}
and
M\leq M_{*}
, we have the main estimate

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}R\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\mathcal{C}_{*,p}\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}\mathcal{M}\left(N,1,\Upsilon^{\prime},\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)

(A.31)

(vi)

For
N\leq N_{*}-\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}
and
M\leq M_{*}
the error term
E
in (
A.27
) satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}E\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\mathcal{C}_{*,p}\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}\left(\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}\right)^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}\Lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,.

(A.32)

Part 4: Nonlocal assumptions and output

(i)

Let
K_{\circ}
be a positive integer, and let
N_{\circ},M_{\circ}
be integers such that

1\leq M_{\circ}\leq N_{\circ}\leq\nicefrac{{M_{*}}}{{2}}\,.

(A.33)

Assume that in addition to the bound (
A.23b
) we have the following global lossy estimates

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{v}\lambda^{\prime N}\nu^{\prime M}

(A.34)

for all
M\leq M_{\circ}
and
N+M\leq N_{\circ}+M_{\circ}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{v}\lambda^{\prime}\lesssim\nu^{\prime}\,.

(A.35)

(ii)

Assume that
{\mathsf{d}}
is large enough so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\mathcal{C}_{*,p}\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{4}}}(\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2})^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{4}}}\Lambda^{n+2+K_{\circ}}\left(1+\frac{\max\{\nu^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{v}\Lambda\}}{\nu}\right)^{M_{\circ}}\leq 1\,.

(A.36)

Then there exists a vector field
\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}=\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)
such that

\displaystyle E=\mathrm{div}\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}+\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}G\varrho\circ\Phi\,dx=:\mathrm{div}\left(\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)\right)+\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}G\varrho\circ\Phi\,dx\,.

(A.37)

In addition, for
N\leq N_{\circ}
and
M\leq M_{\circ}
,
\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}
satisfies the bounds

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{\Lambda^{K_{\circ}}}\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{4}}}(\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2})^{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{4}}}\Lambda^{N}\nu^{M}\,.

(A.38)

Remark A.8

(
Estimates for
\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}
).

Our choice of parameters implies that

\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\overline{R}_{\rm nonlocal}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-10}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}{\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{N}\tau_{q}^{-M}

(A.39)

for
N,M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
. By choosing
N_{\circ}=M_{\circ}=2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}
and
K_{\circ}
large enough so that
\lambda_{q}^{-K_{\circ}}\leq\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-100}
, which follows from (
7.11
). We refer to
[
25
, Remark A.14]
for further details.

Remark A.9

(
Mean of the error term).

From
[
25
, Remark A.17]
, under the same choice of parameters suggested in Remark
A.8
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{d^{M}}{dt^{M}}\langle G(\varrho\circ\Phi)\rangle\bigg|\leq\lambda_{q+\bar{n}}^{-10}\delta_{q+3\bar{n}}^{2}\mathrm{T}_{q+\bar{n}}^{4\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}\tau_{q}^{-M}\qquad\qquad\textnormal{for $M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm ind}$}\,.

Remark A.10

(
Inverse divergence with pointwise bounds).

From
[
25
, Remark A.19]
, if in addition there exists a smooth, non-negative function
\pi
such that

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)

(A.40)

for
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
, then we may additionally conclude that for
N\leq N_{*}-\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}\rfloor
and
M\leq M_{*}
,

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}H^{\alpha_{(j)}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi\left(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,.

(A.41)

Remark A.11

(
Special case for negligible error terms).

The inverse divergence operator defined in the proposition can be applied to an input without the structure of low and high frequency parts when
\varrho=1
and
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}
are sufficiently small. More precisely, we
keep the low-frequency assumptions (Part 1), replace the high-frequency assumptions (Part 2) with
\varrho=1
, and set
\Upsilon=\Upsilon^{\prime}=\Lambda=\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{C}_{*,p}=1
,
{\mathsf{d}}=0
. Then as long as
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}
satisfies (
A.36
), the conclusions in Part 4 hold. In particular,
\mathcal{R}^{*}G
satisfies

\displaystyle G=\mathrm{div}\mathcal{R}^{*}G+\fint_{T^{3}}G\,dx\,.

We require the following variant of the inverse divergence operator; details may be found in
[
25
, Lemma A.22]
.

Lemma A.12

(
Inverse divergence without flow map).

Fix dimension
n\geq 2
. Let
G
be a smooth scalar field and let
{\mathsf{d}}
be a non-negative integer such that there exists a smooth scalar field
\varrho
and tensor field
\vartheta
defined on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
and satisfying
\varrho=\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{\mathsf{d}}}\vartheta^{(i_{1}\dots i_{\mathsf{d}})}(x)
.

Part 1: Algorithm for inverse divergence

We have a decomposition

\displaystyle G\varrho=:\mathrm{div}(\mathcal{H}(G\varrho))+E

(A.42)

where the vector field
\mathcal{H}(G\varrho)
and scalar field
E
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(G\varrho)^{\bullet}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=0}^{{\mathsf{d}}-1}(-1)^{{\mathsf{d}}-k+1}\partial_{i_{k+2}}\dots\partial_{i_{{\mathsf{d}}}}G\,\underbrace{\mathrm{div}^{(k)}}_{{\partial_{i_{1}},\dots,\partial_{i_{k}}}}\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{k},\bullet,i_{k+2},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})},\quad E=(-1)^{\mathsf{d}}\nabla^{{\mathsf{d}}}G:\vartheta\,,

(A.43)

where we use the convention
\partial_{i_{k+2}}\cdots\partial_{i_{{\mathsf{d}}}}G=G
and
\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{k},\bullet,i_{k+2},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}=\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{{\mathsf{d}}-1},\bullet)}
when
k={\mathsf{d}}-1
.

Part 2: Localized assumptions and output

Fix a set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. Let parameters
N_{*}\geq M_{*}\geq 1
be given. Define
v
and
D_{t}
as in Part 1 of Proposition
A.7
, where
v
satisfies (
A.23b
) with
\lambda^{\prime},\nu,\nu^{\prime},N_{*},M_{*}
and
L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)
replaced with
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. Let smooth, non-negative functions
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be given such that

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\qquad\textnormal{on }\Omega\,

(A.44a)

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{{\mathsf{d}}-k}\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{k}}\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi^{\prime}\Lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\qquad\textnormal{on }\Omega

(A.44b)

for
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
, where the parameters satisfy

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime},\lambda\leq\Upsilon\leq\Lambda,\quad\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Upsilon^{-1}\leq 1,\quad N_{*}\geq{\mathsf{d}},\quad\lambda,\nu,\nu^{\prime}\geq 1\,.

(A.45)

Then
\mathcal{H}(G\varrho)
satisfies

\mathrm{supp\,}(\mathcal{H}(G\varrho))\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}(G\vartheta)\,,

(A.46)

and for
N\leq N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}
and
M\leq M_{*}
,

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\mathcal{H}(G\varrho)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi\pi^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-1}\Lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,\quad\textnormal{on }\Omega\,.

(A.47)

Part 3: Nonlocal assumptions and output

Finally, we assume that all assumptions from (
i
) in Part 4 in Proposition
A.7
hold. Next, we assume that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\lambda^{N}(\nu^{\prime})^{M}\,,

(A.48a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\partial_{i_{1}}\dots\partial_{i_{k}}\vartheta^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\mathsf{d}})}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*,\infty}\Upsilon^{k-{\mathsf{d}}}\Lambda^{N}(\nu^{\prime})^{M}\,

(A.48b)

for
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
. Also, we choose
{\mathsf{d}}
large enough to satisfy

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\mathcal{C}_{*,\infty}(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Upsilon^{-1})^{{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}}\Lambda^{K_{\circ}}\left(1+\frac{\max\{\nu^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{v}\Lambda\}}{\nu}\right)^{M_{\circ}}\ \leq 1\,.

(A.49)

Then we may write

\displaystyle E=:\mathrm{div}\left(\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho)\right)+\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}G\varrho\,dx\,,

(A.50)

where
\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho)
is a vector field which for
N\leq N_{\circ}
and
M\leq M_{\circ}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Lambda^{K_{\circ}}}(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Upsilon^{-1})^{{\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}}}\Lambda^{N}\nu^{M}\,.

(A.51)

A.5 Abstract construction of intermittent pressure

In this subsection, we recall the abstract construction of an intermittent pressure which can be used to dominate the inverse divergence (see the preceding subsection) of an error term
G\varrho\circ\Phi
. For the proof we refer the reader to
[
25
, Proposition 7.5]
.

Proposition A.13

(
Pressure increment and upgrade error from current error).

Part 1: Preliminary assumptions

(i)

There exists a scalar field
G
, constants
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}
for
p=1,\infty
, and parameters
M_{t},\lambda,\nu,\nu^{\prime},N_{*},M_{*}
such that (
A.20
) and (
A.21
) are satisfied. There exists
\pi
smooth and non-negative and
r_{G}
such that

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\pi^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{G}^{-1}\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\nu^{\prime}\right)\,.

(ii)

There exists an incompressible vector field
v
with material derivative
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
, a volume preserving diffeomorphism
\Phi
, inverse flow
\Phi^{-1}
, and parameter
\lambda^{\prime}
such that (
A.22
)–(
A.23b
) are satisfied.

(iii)

There exists a zero mean scalar function
\varrho
, a mean-zero tensor potential
\vartheta
, constants
\mathcal{C}_{*,p}
for
p=1,\infty
, and parameters
\mu,{\Upsilon,\Upsilon^{\prime}},\Lambda,{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}},{\mathsf{d}}
such that (
i
)–(
iii
) of Part 2 in Proposition
A.7
and (
A.24
)–(
A.26
) are satisfied.

(iv)

The current error
\varphi=\mathcal{H}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)
and nonlocal error
E
satisfy the conclusions in (
A.27
), (
ii
)–(
vi
) of Part 3 in Proposition
A.7
, as well as (
A.41
) from Remark
A.10
with
\pi
replaced by
\pi^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{G}^{-1}
.

(v)

There exist integers
N_{\circ},M_{\circ},K_{\circ}
such that (
A.33
)–(
A.36
) hold, which in turn imply (
A.37
)–(
A.38
).

Part 2: Additional assumptions

(i)

There exists a large positive integer
N_{**}
and positive integers
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x},\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
such that

\displaystyle N_{*}-2{\mathsf{d}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-N_{**}-3

\displaystyle\geq M_{*}\,,

(A.52a)

\displaystyle M_{*}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1\geq 2N_{\circ}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad{M_{t}>\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\,,

(A.52b)

\displaystyle N_{**}

\displaystyle\geq{2{\mathsf{d}}}+3

(A.52c)

(ii)

There exist parameters
\Gamma=\Lambda^{\alpha}
for
0<\alpha\ll 1
,
\delta_{\rm tiny},r_{\phi}
, and
\delta_{\phi,p}
for
p=1,\infty
satisfying

\displaystyle 0<r_{\phi}\leq 1\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\phi,p}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}=\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\mathcal{C}_{\ast,p}{\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}r_{\phi}\,,

(A.53a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}\,,

(A.53b)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}+1\right)\left({\mathcal{C}_{*,\infty}\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}+1\right)\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}

\displaystyle\leq{\delta_{\rm tiny}}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\,,\mathcal{C}_{G,1}\,,{\mathcal{C}_{*,1}\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}\,,

(A.53c)

\displaystyle\qquad 2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4

\displaystyle\leq N_{*}-N_{**}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-3{\mathsf{d}}-3\,,

(A.53d)

\displaystyle(\Lambda\Gamma)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\mu}{2\pi\sqrt{3}\Gamma\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})}\right)^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}\,.

(A.53e)

(iii)

There exists a parameter
\bar{m}
and an increasing sequence of frequencies
\{\mu_{0},\cdots,\mu_{\bar{m}}\}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mu<\mu_{0}

\displaystyle<\cdots<\mu_{\bar{m}-1}\leq\Lambda<\Lambda\Gamma<\mu_{\bar{m}}\,,

(A.54a)

\displaystyle\max(\lambda,

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma\left(\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\mu_{m}+{\mu}^{-1}\right)\leq 1\,,

(A.54b)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,1}\mathcal{C}_{*,1}r_{\phi}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}

\displaystyle\nu\Gamma(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}\left(\max\left({\mu}^{-1},\mu_{m}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\right)\right)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad\times(\mu_{\bar{m}})^{5+K_{\circ}}\left(1+\frac{\max\{\nu^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{v}\mu_{\bar{m}}\}}{\nu}\right)^{M_{\circ}}\leq 1\,,

(A.54c)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,1}\mathcal{C}_{*,1}r_{\phi}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\nu\Gamma

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\Lambda\Gamma}{\mu_{\bar{m}}}\right)^{N_{**}}(\mu_{\bar{m}})^{8+K_{\circ}}\left(1+\frac{\max\{\nu^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{v}\mu_{\bar{m}}\}}{\nu}\right)^{M_{\circ}}\leq 1\,,

(A.54d)

for all
{1\leq m\leq\bar{m}}
.

Part 3: Pressure increment

Define the pressure increment
\sigma_{\phi}
by

\displaystyle\sigma_{\phi}

\displaystyle=r_{\phi}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\mathsf{\Pi}(H)\left(\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)\circ\Phi-\langle\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)\rangle\right)\,,\qquad\qquad\sigma_{\phi}^{+}:=r_{\phi}^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\mathsf{\Pi}(H)\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)\circ\Phi\,,

(A.55a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Pi}(H)

\displaystyle:=\left((\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}})^{2}+\sum_{N=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\sum_{M=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}(\lambda\Gamma)^{-2N}(\nu\Gamma)^{-2M}|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}H|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}-(\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}})^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\,,

(A.55b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Pi}(\rho)

\displaystyle:=\left((\mathcal{C}_{\rho,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}})^{2}+\sum_{N=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}(\Lambda\Gamma)^{-2N}|D^{N}\rho|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}-(\mathcal{C}_{\rho,\infty}\Gamma^{-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}})^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\,,

(A.55c)

(i)

\sigma_{\varphi}
has a synthetic Littlewood-Paley decomposition

\sigma_{\varphi}=\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}-\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}=\sigma_{\varphi}^{*}+\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}\sigma_{\varphi}^{m}\,.

(A.56)

(ii)

\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}
dominates derivatives of
\varphi
with suitable weights, so that for all
N\leq N_{*}-\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}\rfloor
,
M\leq M_{*}
,

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\varphi\right|\lesssim\left((\sigma_{\varphi}^{+})^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}r_{\phi}^{-1}+\delta_{\rm tiny}\right)\left(\Lambda\Gamma\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,.

(A.57)

(iii)

\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}
dominates derivatives of itself with suitable weights, so that for all
N\leq N_{*}-\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}\rfloor-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
,
M\leq M_{*}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
,

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}+\delta_{\rm tiny})\left(\Lambda\Gamma\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t},\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,.

(A.58)

(iv)

\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}
and
\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}
have size comparable to
\varphi
, so that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}},\,\left\|\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{\phi,1}\,,\qquad\left\|\sigma_{\varphi}^{+}\right\|_{\infty},\,\left\|\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta_{\phi,\infty}\,.

(A.59)

(v)

\pi
dominates derivatives of
\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}
with suitable weights, so that for all
N\leq N_{*}-\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{2}}\rfloor-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
,
M\leq M_{*}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
,

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\sigma_{\varphi}^{-}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{r_{\phi}}{r_{G}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left({\mathcal{C}_{*,1}{\Upsilon^{-2}}\Upsilon^{\prime}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\pi(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t},\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,.

(A.60)

(vi)

We have the support properties

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{+})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}(\varphi)\,,\quad\mathrm{supp\,}(\sigma_{\varphi}^{-})\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}(G)\,.

(A.61)

Part 4: Current error

(i)

There exists a current error
\phi_{\varphi}
, where we have the decomposition and equalities

\displaystyle\phi_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\phi_{\varphi}^{*}+\sum_{m=0}^{\bar{m}}\phi_{\varphi}^{m}

(A.62a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}\phi^{\bullet}_{\varphi}(t,x)

\displaystyle=D_{t}\sigma^{\bullet}_{\varphi}(t,x)-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}D_{t}\sigma_{\varphi}^{\bullet}(t,x^{\prime})\,dx^{\prime}\,,\qquad\bullet=m,*\,.

(A.62b)

(ii)

\phi^{m}_{\varphi}
can be written as
\phi^{m}_{\varphi}=\phi^{m,l}_{\varphi}+\phi^{m,*}_{\varphi}
and for
1\leq m\leq\bar{m}
these satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\phi_{\varphi}^{m}\right\|_{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\nu\Gamma^{2}\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,1}\mathcal{C}_{*,1}{\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}r_{\phi}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\mu_{m}}\left(\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,,

(A.63a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\phi_{\varphi}^{m}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\nu\Gamma^{2}\left(\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}\mathcal{C}_{*,1}{\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}r_{\phi}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\frac{\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)}{\mu}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\mu_{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,,

(A.63b)

\displaystyle\left|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\phi_{\varphi}^{m,l}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\nu\Gamma^{2}\pi\left(\frac{r_{\phi}}{r_{G}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{*,1}{\Upsilon^{\prime}\Upsilon^{-2}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}}\left(\frac{\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)}{\mu}\right)^{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\mu_{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)\,,

(A.63c)

for all
N\leq N_{*}-2{\mathsf{d}}-{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}
,
M\leq M_{*}-{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}-1
. For
m=0
and the same range of
N
and
M
,
\phi_{\varphi}^{m}
and
\phi_{\varphi}^{m,l}
satisfy identical bounds but with
\mu_{m-1}^{2}\mu_{m}
replaced with
{\Gamma\mu^{-1}}
and
\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)
replaced with
\mu_{0}
in all three bounds. Furthermore, for all
N\leq N_{\circ},M\leq M_{\circ}
, the nonlocal and remainder portions satisfy the improved estimates

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\phi_{\varphi}^{m,*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\min(\mu_{m},\Lambda\Gamma)\right)^{N-K_{\circ}}{(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}\left(\max\left({\mu}^{-1},\mu_{m}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\right)\right)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}}(\nu\Gamma)^{M}\,,

(A.64)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\phi_{\varphi}^{*}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Lambda\Gamma)^{-K_{\circ}}{(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}\left(\max\left({\mu}^{-1},\mu_{m}\mu_{m-1}^{-2}\right)\right)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}}\left(\Lambda\Gamma\right)^{N}(\nu\Gamma)^{M}\,.

(A.65)

(iii)

We have the support properties

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}(\phi^{m,l}_{\varphi})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}G\cap B\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\vartheta,2\mu_{m-1}^{-1}\right)\circ\Phi\,\textnormal{ for }\,1\leq m\leq\bar{m}\,,\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\phi_{\varphi}^{0,l}\right)\subseteq\mathrm{supp\,}G\,.

(A.66)

(iv)

For all
M\leq M_{*}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1
, we have that the mean
\langle D_{t}\sigma_{S}\rangle
satisfies

\left|\frac{d^{M}}{dt^{M}}\langle D_{t}\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\right|\lesssim(\Lambda\Gamma)^{-K_{\circ}}{(\max(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\Gamma)^{\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}\mu^{-\lfloor\nicefrac{{{\mathsf{d}}}}{{{4}}}\rfloor}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-1,\nu\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\Gamma\right)

(A.67)
